The UX substrate

What a CivicOS member sees and works with, as the approved requirements and Bob's rulings define it today, and everything a designer needs to build its experience on. It opens with the decisions that are still Bob's to make.

Part 1 · For Bob

What a member does with CivicOS

A group runs its own copy of CivicOS to investigate how a government is meeting its obligations. Members bring documents in with a record of where each came from. They ask questions and support each answer with passages from those documents. A project concludes a question on the support it accepts, and the concluded question becomes a finding. The group publishes its findings as a case, in editions.

Then the group decides what to do. From the first suspicion, a project can open an action plan: the matters it is about and how well each is shown, the options open to it (a records request, a complaint, a story, testimony, a filing through counsel, and so on), which it chooses and which it declines with a reason, and up to three scenarios with checkpoints the group judges. Choosing an option starts an action, addressed to an office, an outlet or an organisation, never a private person. The assistant may draft what is sent; a member approves it and sends it by their own hand. The group then tracks replies and deadlines and, for a breach, the seven-stage escalation until compliance is restored.

Three things run through all of it. The record always says what is not known and why. It keeps track of who looked where and what they found. And the assistant finds, proposes and drafts, but a person makes every judgement and takes every act that commits the group.

flowchart TB
  subgraph E["1 · Evidence"]

  subgraph J["2 · Judgement"]
    direction LR
    q["Question"] --> bv["Account of support"] --> prj["Project concludes"] --> fnd["Finding"]
  end
  subgraph P["3 · Publication and action"]
    direction LR
    cs["Case, published"] --> det["Determination against a standard"] --> plan["Action plan: options and scenarios"] --> act["Action, sent by a member"] --> mon["Tracking replies, deadlines, escalation"]
  end
  leg --> q
  fnd --> cs
  q -.->|a plan may open early| plan
  str["Strength and declared bias"] -.->|weigh| q
  ai["Assistant"] -.->|finds and checks; never concludes| leg
  qu["Queue"] -.->|what waits on each member| prj

In the diagrams below, colour shows how far each thing is built: Built Built, with gaps Not built

How much of it has a screen today

The requirements imply 38 screens. In the old interface 8 exist, 14 exist in part, and 16 were never built. Of the 230 acts a member or administrator can take, 158 have no control in the old interface. The redesign starts from the requirements, not from the old screens.

What is still needed from you

These are the decisions about what members and readers see and do that only you can make. Each one opens to a full brief, written to stand on its own: what you are asked to decide; the basics you need, explained from scratch; how it works today; the problem and why it matters; a worked example with named people; what is already fixed and why; the choices, each traced through the same example with its gains, costs and what it commits you to; the trade-off in a sentence; a recommendation with its main risk; sample replies; and, at the end, the words used. Take them in any order; the table shows which are quick.

To answer, reply with the number and your ruling, for example “25: as recommended” or “34: Matters”. A question is just as welcome as a ruling. Each ruling is recorded, folded into the design documents, and this page is updated in the same change.

Nothing is waiting on you. Every question on this page is settled; see what is settled. A new question appears here, with its brief, when one arises.

    Folded into another question: 17 (How an office named in a case sees it and replies) into 36.

    Left to the design

    These are open, but they are detail within rules already set, so the designer settles them without you. Say so if you want any of them brought to you.

    • Scenarios side by side or as tabs on narrow screens: PATH §4. The approved plan page shows tabs ('A · Negotiate first', 'B · File now') and a disabled '+ scenario' at three. build/plan/action-design/PATH.md §4; build/plan/action-design/plan-page.html (approved by Bob, K608 (4))
    • Drawing a branch that waits on another subject's track without implying a forecast: PATH §4 ('when the certification's escalation reaches legal tools'). build/plan/action-design/PATH.md §4; action-plans R15
    • Default reminder days offered when a dated option is chosen: action-plans R29: 'the defaults are the surface's, and nothing here sets one'; shown and changeable, nothing preselected unseen (DEC-77). action-plans R29; DEC-94 (1); DEC-77
    • How a nearing deadline changes position, colour or wording: DEC-94 (2) allows display change only; threshold looks and wording are Design's. DEC-94; ux-experience OQ-14 ruled entry
    • The disposition chip for an undecided option: The approved view prints the code word 'open'; PATH and ruling 7 say 'undecided'. Also 'done' and 'blocked' have no drawn form yet. build/plan/action-design/plan-page.html (approved by Bob, K608 (4)); PATH.md §2 step 6; action-plans R13
    • Where the matter page sits: A design view gathers determination, consequences, escalation, actions and pressure for one act; the inventory has separate Escalation, Standards and determinations, and Consequences screens. build/plan/action-design/matter-page.html (design view, not stated as approved)
    • The hostile-response check's wording: action-plans R19 names the check; the view says 'no branch answers a hostile response (refusal, obstruction, retaliation)'. action-plans R19; build/plan/action-design/plan-page.html (approved by Bob, K608 (4))
    • Empty and loading states of the plan page, tray and Start preview: Not specified anywhere (as for other surfaces, open question 15). ux-experience OQ-15
    • How 'describe it to the assistant, which fills the form' looks: PATH step 4; the assistant panel's look is DEC-90's (docked, never floating). build/plan/action-design/PATH.md §2 step 4; DEC-90

    What is settled

    The design as Bob has ruled it, stated as it now stands. Where a ruling left part of a question open, the rest is under What is still needed from you.

    Words

    What “rung” means to a member

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K356): “Rung” is the weight of an act, shown on every button: can be undone, needs a reason, ends something, signed, cannot be undone. The sequence from releasing work to publishing it (release, stand behind, ground, conclude, accept, ratify, publish) is called the path to publication.

    What “finding” means to a member

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K356): Finding means a concluded question. The queue's class of items that the record noticed and nobody has judged yet is shown to members as Noticed.

    A project shows its stage

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K356, K362): A project shows one of four stages (forming, investigating, matured, closed) on its home screen. Three are computed from the questions it holds and what it has published, never set by hand; closing is the owner's recorded act with a reason.

    The design's questions

    1 Contradictions: how they are shown and resolved

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-76, DEC-77, DEC-84, DEC-85): A contradiction is conditional: two assertions that cannot both hold in the same respect. Resolving one means finding the respect in which they differ, finding the side that is wrong, or recording that the conflict is genuine. A candidate a member takes up becomes an inquiry whose conclusion records its kind (dissolved, corrected, or genuine; double-speak becomes a finding, and an obligation set against an act goes to conformance). It is shown inline wherever the two points already sit, through a tension mark and a short 'how do these differ' clarifier, with a dedicated contradiction screen only as the escalation; context may fill in facts and recommend judgements, labelled, but never silently decides. An undetermined candidate is a lead, never a duty; a record duty binds the joined members of every project drawing on either side until it is resolved; two projects' different conclusions are plurality unless they cannot both hold in the same respect, when a tension mark asks how they differ; the machine recommends only which respects may differ; a hypothesised cause is published as 'cause not established'; publication discloses, never blocks; 'one of them is wrong' needs a written reason; a dismissal gives one fixed reason. When the other side is in a project a member cannot see, each project is told of the conflict on its own side only; a checkbox signals willingness to resolve it, and when both projects opt in their members respond through relayed notices, sharing only what each chooses; a conflict remaining at publication is highlighted without naming the hidden project (DEC-85).

    2 A knock: how a tip becomes part of the record

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-78, DEC-88): Pulling a knock admits its material as a capture at 'collected' carrying the knock's receipt, and commits the member to nothing more; vouching stays with release. The source is recorded as an unnamed knocker identified by the receipt (never 'unknown'), the pulling member is the capturing actor and never the source, a knocker may prove continuity with a secret they keep, and a source's identity is held as a dated history of disclosures, published only with consent. The note travels with the capture as the knocker's own words; the contact stays in the inbox. The inbox act is regraded reasoned once pulling admits material (DEC-88).

    3 A project's stage and its cases' readiness

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-79, K362, K364, K904): All four stages are shown: 'forming', 'investigating' and 'matured' are computed from the record, and 'closed' is the owner's reasoned act. Only readiness rungs a case can earn are shown, and each unearned one is stated. The display is coloured bars that build up, one per stage reached, disclosing more on hover (with a focus or tap equivalent) and opening on click to what each stage needs next and when each was reached; each bar is named, and 'closed' shows its reason and never reads as a finished stack.

    4 The publication ceremony

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-80, DEC-81): There is no placeholder: the ceremony and its pre-flight are designed and built in the redesign, in five steps re-derived for DEC-72 (step three discloses any unresolved record contradiction and asks consent for any source identity revealed), and an owner signs in the browser with a registered browser-held key producing the same sshsig signature. A published case may rest on a co-attested Grade B capture, each document's grade and co-attestation disclosed; one whose co-attestation failed may be published only by an attributed acknowledgement and is marked 'self-attested only' (DEC-81). Grade A and further co-witnesses are deferred, their options documented in GRADE-A-CAPTURE.md (DEC-81 item 4).

    5 Strength and grades in the project workspace

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-82): The project workspace shows strength: each question shows its capture and connection badges (with testimony beside them when an account is part of the answer) and one plain phrase against the project's bar, such as 'Short on connection · bar B/B', 'Undetermined' named with its reason, never one combined score. Every scale follows the measures map Bob approved (letters grade evidence, bars show progress, weights mark acts), docs/development/ux-substrate/measures-map.html, rendered at https://claude.ai/artifact/TfqcXNaJQ86SZzUA8Xn6Ni.

    6 “Undetermined” and its four neighbours

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-86): 'Undetermined' is one component with a fixed mark and a line that always says 'because…', the same everywhere. 'Withheld', 'Unrated', 'Nobody looked' and 'Refused' each have their own visibly different treatment and one fixed sentence pattern, never shared with each other or with Undetermined. None is dressed as an error, and all are written as named terms (DEC-82). The exact marks and wording are the redesign's, within this rule.

    7 How the weight of an act is felt

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-87, DEC-88): Friction is kept as low as possible so the tool fades and the work stays in focus, while members still feel the weight of their work and the finality of some steps; cues rich in information show state, relationships, context, progression and the acts available; everything is self-evident to newcomers and intuitive to experienced members; and a heavier act slows the member down, even for a moment. Friction follows the rung: a reversible act happens inline; a reasoned act opens a reason field in place; a terminal or attested act opens a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs; an irreversible act goes only through the ceremony. Every act's button shows its rung's name and weight mark, and an act whose rung is not yet set is treated as reasoned. The 57 acts that had no rung have Bob's (26 reversible, 29 reasoned, 2 terminal, DEC-88). Six judgement calls carry high friction whatever their rung: choosing a person's attribution, sharing a lead, registering a subject and setting the group's strength bar open the full dialog stating their effect; approving a filing is terminal and has it already; having the assistant work an objective, the lightest of the six, opens its reason field in place with the run's budget and scope beside it. A heavy consequence is not a new rung.

    8 Where the judgement to pursue a matter, or not, is recorded

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-89, action-plans R13, escalation R13): Whether a matter is worth pursuing is recorded only in acts and their reasons, never in a score. Opening an escalation takes a written reason. 'Decline to escalate' on a live noncompliant determination records, in the member's own words, why the group is not pursuing it now: reasoned, attributed and dated, corrected only forward (a later escalation, opened with its own reason, supersedes it and both stay readable). In an action plan, not pursuing a matter is an option declined with a short reason. No field, value or vocabulary for significance, severity, priority, urgency or rank exists anywhere. Any joined member who may open an escalation may decline one.

    9 The action plan page

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (Bob's rulings 1-10 of 2026-09-29 (K590), D1-D6 and the closing rule of 2026-09-30 (K590), plan-page view approved (K608 (4)), UX-ANSWERS.md): The plan page is an outline, not a graph: its subjects with their support; its options collapsed to a one-line summary, category and dates, sortable by category, date, subject or disposition, with declined options kept visible with their reasons; up to three scenarios of phases with checkpoints and branches that a member judges; and what each started option became. A strip under the title shows the checks in words. The assistant's suggestions wait in a separate tray until adopted. It holds no costs, budgets, assignees or scores and is never published. A graph view may come later. Layout detail remains Design's. The approved sketch is views/plan-page.html.

    10 The assistant panel

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-90): the assistant is a panel docked beside the surface it serves, and it never floats. Its text carries the one 'machine work' treatment every machine output shares, with attribution ('the assistant did this, at <member>'s request'); a wizard step highlights the real control and never fills or presses it, and the member performs every act on the real surface. Two refinements: the panel expands (a draggable divider and an 'expand' control let it take most of the window while some of the member's surface stays visible; it shrinks back by itself when a wizard step needs the member to act; its size is remembered, and one click restores the default), and it docks by screen shape, at the side on wide screens and at the bottom on narrow ones, with the member free to switch edges and the choice remembered.

    11 What a newcomer learns first

    Deferred by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-91): Deferred: 'this needs to be deferred until the new UX is in place and stabilized.' Onboarding is designed against the product members will actually use. Meanwhile the measures map already rules that a newcomer meets strength against the bar and 'Undetermined' first, everything else taught at the act (DEC-82).

    Comes back when: The redesigned member surfaces are built and have been stable in use (no major layout changes) for a period BOB judges sufficient; or Bob asks.

    12 Where information from other groups came from (the origin mark)

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-92, DEC-96): Information from other groups carries an origin mark in the same family as 'Machine work', answering who made this: Ours; Another group's (a published edition, signature verified); Accepted by our group; 'Not yet evaluated'; and 'Flagged'. The mark is ambient, a hover shows the fact behind it, and it is never composed with grades or strength into one trust score. Accepting another group's work is a reasoned act naming one edition (a newer edition reads 'Another group's' until accepted); it records who, when and why, changes no grade, and is withdrawn by a reasoned act that sends re-evaluation notices. A flag is a member's recorded evaluation naming the specific issue, never a machine's; it stays inside the group and is cleared by a member with a reason. 'Meets standards' stays deferred until an evaluator of incoming work exists, and if it returns it is labelled machine work. When a group's published case relies on another group's work, the case states the acceptance and discloses any open flag on it: disclosed, never blocked. A public list of acceptances and flags follows when a second group asks to see who accepted or flagged its work.

    13 Telling a member a capture's grade

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-95, DEC-97): The whole grade note is shown once, when a capture completes; a capture that completes later and unattended carries the note on the completed capture and on its queue item. Captures still held (collected, not yet released or set aside) are listed in a 'Held captures' view, per member and per project, showing only what the viewer may see, sortable by age, source and project, each row with its age, and never notified. A member ticks several (nothing pre-ticked) and may vouch for them together as one batch release (each document its own entry, with the member's recorded statement of how uniform the batch is and what was sampled), set them aside together with one reason (each stays held, never deleted), or link them to a question. Crucial documents, and documents caught in an unresolved contradiction, are shown as not eligible, with the reason, before the member acts. The system may also suggest that a held capture bears on a member's work, under six guards: it appears where the member already works, never as a notification; it is labelled machine work with its reason; a dismissal is remembered; it never appears during a heavy act; a member may switch it off; and its acceptance rate is measured.

    14 Reminders without nagging

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-10, DEC-69, DEC-70, DEC-94): A deadline reminder is the member's own request, never the system's nagging: it is set up when the option is chosen in the action plan, from defaults the member sees and can change there, and any further reminder is one the member accepts, perhaps at the system's suggestion. As a deadline nears, the item's position, colour or wording may change; that is display, not a notification. Nothing reaches a member who has not opened the product: no email, push or other outside channel. An overdue deadline notifies once; 'due within N days' is not new unless the member asked for that reminder. An overdue condition's response offers another reminder at a further time, or none (DEC-10). A pushed notice tells once, is dispositionable and ages (DEC-70); the workflow never second-guesses (DEC-69). Threshold looks and wording are Design's.

    15 What screens say while empty, loading or waiting

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-86, DEC-94, DEC-98): Empty, loading, failed and waiting states share one vocabulary across every screen, each with its own fixed look and none dressed as an error: 'Nothing here' (truly empty); 'Still loading'; 'Could not read this, because…' (the screen's own request failed); and the record's 'Undetermined, because…'. A screen that cannot tell 'could not read' from 'none' says 'could not read'. Every wait carries one line naming what is awaited, from whom, and the expected or legal date where one exists ('Waiting on the City Clerk's reply, due 14 October'), or that no date is set. An empty screen offers one plain next step only on something the member has just created (a new project, a new plan: 'Nothing here yet. Add the first question.'), nowhere else. As a deadline nears, an item's position, colour or wording may change; that is display, not a notification. Exact wording and looks are the redesign's.

    16 Accessibility standard and languages

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-99): Every member screen of the redesign, and the published case page, print and file, is built to WCAG 2.2 at level AA, and each new screen is checked against it before it is accepted; the rules already set (colour never the only signal, focus and tap equivalents to hover, a docked assistant panel) are part of meeting it. The interface is in English for now, built so its words live in one place and another language can be added without rebuilding screens; a published case appears in the language its group wrote it in. A translation of a published case is never presented as the signed case: it is marked as an unofficial translation and names the signed original. Whether and how a group issues translations, and the interface in a place's languages, are decided later.

    Comes back when: A group asks to publish a translated case, or to use CivicOS in another language.

    18 What changed in a new edition, and following other groups' editions

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-101): Every edition after the first carries a required statement, 'What changed in this edition, and why', shown at the top of the new edition and quoted where the older edition points to it; a new edition cannot be signed without it. The assistant may draft it: an initial, perhaps partial, detailed high-level description of the changes and, as far as the system can tell, the motivation for the revision, never a diff. The draft is labelled machine work until a member adopts or rewrites it, and the record keeps that it began as a machine draft. A group's copy can be configured to watch the published cases of other groups it cites or follows, as it watches documents for updates, and to respond to a new edition by telling the members whose work rests on the cited edition; nothing is pushed between groups.

    19 How members are credited, and what anonymity does to testimony

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-102): A member chooses how a published case credits their observation, at one of four levels: the group, the project, their cover, or by name ('by name' publishes their handle, never a legal name). An edition is not signed while a member whose observation it uses has not chosen; the owner's way forward is to drop the finding that rests on it. The member is asked by a to-do in their own queue, and the state shows on the case draft. Every level appears publicly in one neutral style with the grade beside it. The level also sets the testimony's weight: testimony credited at the group or project level is anonymous and is treated like an anonymous tip, potentially weaker because a member may have a vested interest, and must be corroborated to journalistic and legal standards before a finding rests on it; identified at cover or name, it may carry greater strength. A change of level reaches the findings resting on it as re-evaluation notices.

    20 How a group's declared bias appears to the public

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-103): Readers see the lens itself. At publication every statement in force is printed into the signed case: its kind in plain words, its subject, its text, and its justification and evidence beneath, citing only public material and stating how many citations are withheld. The published page shows 'The lens this case was produced under': the publisher's acknowledgement first, then each statement with its reasons, then two lines on why groups declare bias; collapsed on screen to one line per statement with its justification, always full in print. The publisher sees exactly what will be printed before signing. Earlier cases keep only the manifest's fingerprint.

    21 What a hunch's grade does, and clearing hunches before publishing

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-15, DEC-20, DEC-80, DEC-104): A hunch is temporary declared bias: visible as a hunch everywhere from the moment it is made, with its author and date. It links the evidence it joins, for traversal, search and suggestion, but counts for nothing in any strength reading, and strength says how many hunches it left out. The hunch's letter appears only on the link itself, in the hunch style, as the member's stated confidence when the guess was made, beside 'not counted'. A 'Hunches to clear' list sits on the question and project pages, as status where the work lives and never a notification; the check before publishing, before the ceremony's first screen, names every hunch still standing, and a case cannot be published while one stands.

    22 Standards of proof by audience: the project's bar

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-72, DEC-81, DEC-105): The bar belongs to a project and never combines across projects. The owner sets it now: a letter per axis, or none, each with its one-line meaning from the measures map, under the line 'CivicOS has no guidance yet on what particular audiences expect. Readers see the bar you set in these words.' When audience guidance comes, it takes the form already ruled for legal venues: sourced audience standards in the jurisdiction profile, shown beside the choice, reading 'Undetermined' where nobody has researched them, never preselected or defaulted. The research starts when a group asks what bar suits an audience, or a case is challenged as below its audience's standard.

    Comes back when: A group asks what bar suits an audience, or a case is challenged as below its audience's standard.

    23 Saying 'there is more you cannot see' about work that depends on this

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-36, K903): The rule for hidden things applies in both directions: a hidden item is never named or counted, and no id, title or state of it is shown, whether it is something the member's work rests on or something that rests on or cites what the member is looking at. Lists of dependents and 'cited by' lists withhold a hidden item whole, and say only that something was withheld, in the 'Withheld' style. The one-for-one placeholders ('an object you may not see') are removed, since they let a reader count hidden items. Ruled by Bob through the development process on his secondary account, 1 October (K903 (4)).

    24 Always knowing which side of the privacy fence you are on

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-19, DEC-80, DEC-106): Members are taught two spaces, each with its own distinct frame: the group's private working record and the public published record. Review copies and outgoing drafts appear inside the working frame with a band saying what they are and who can see them (for example 'Shared for review: 2 people outside the group can read this'). Each document and case carries a small path-to-publication marker showing where it stands ('working → shared for review → published'). Publishing, through the ceremony, is the only crossing. Frames, colours, bands and the marker's look are Design's.

    25 Our own missed deadlines are never findings, and the queue's 'To do'

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (action-plans R23, K611, DEC-107): A plan's checkpoints are the group's own intentions: a missed or not-met checkpoint is never a finding, a condition of the record or a fact about the government, and its reminder reaches the queue as the group's own item. The other side's missed deadline is a signal on the action, raised once when it passes. Members see the queue's to-do class as 'To do' (its internal code unchanged, as with 'Noticed'); on members' and readers' screens 'obligation' means only a public body's duty. Each item's sentence names whose step it is where that matters ('our plan', 'the city's deadline'). How declared flows and their deltas are drawn is Design's.

    26 The doorbell: its inbox, limits, gatekeeper and discards

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-78, DEC-108): The doorbell's inbox reads like an email inbox: unhandled knocks highlighted, handled ones still visible, sortable by time, status, whether the knocker presented a secret, the project affected, and so on. The limits are at most 5 knocks from one source and 10 in total in any 10 minutes, and a would-be knocker is made aware whenever a limit is in effect, so nobody believes they knocked when they did not. A group may enable a gatekeeper (machine work) that identifies and dismisses obvious spam and denial-of-service submissions. Discarded knocks go to an archive, in case a gem is buried in the flood, cleared automatically a week after receipt. The system keeps a count-only tally of knocks it turned away (a daily total for the whole doorbell, how often the whole-doorbell limit was reached, and when last; no addresses, fingerprints or content), shown as status on the inbox page, never a notification; the refusal text says truthfully that the group can see how often its doorbell turns people away.

    27 What the founder is told about who really controls the group's copy

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-2, DEC-109): Before the founder chooses a password, the claim page and the setup wizard's last screen tell the whole truth in a short plain block written as practical steps: whoever can sign in to the hosting account controls the copy (can replace the one-time password, claim the copy again, read everything, lock everyone out, and no administrators' vote can stop them); use a group account, not a personal login; add at least one other trusted person; where possible let someone other than the administrators hold it; and the same account is the way back in if the password is lost. Administrator settings carry a standing 'Who controls this copy' card with the same explanation, who the group recorded as holding hosting access and when, and when the copy was last claimed where it can show it. No acknowledgement is recorded; custody itself stays deferred.

    28 The queue's three kinds of item: names, treatment, sorting

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (K356, DEC-107, DEC-110): The queue is one list. Members see its three kinds as 'To do', 'Noticed' and 'Signal', each with its own icon, wording, buttons and an 'Open…' link to its home (until the flow model and the signal history exist, the link goes to the thing the item concerns). The default order is grouped by case, with to-dos, then noticed items, then signals; a member may re-sort by time added, time due, case or kind, and when sorted by case or kind the list folds into collapsible groups so a member can focus on one. The detailed look is Design's.

    29 The network: believeincities.org, 'working on' notices, and keeping them honest

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-111): The network's directory and forum stay outside CivicOS: after publishing, a member gets links out and a prefilled directory submission, after the warning; nothing is sent automatically. Inside CivicOS, a project's owner may tell the network the group is working on something, but only from a project defined as working on the issue, after a warning that the public, including anyone being examined, will see it and that stopping later won't unsay it. The notice names the group by its slug (never its members; there are no anonymous notices) and carries the owner's own wording, optionally naming the public body and the matter; 'Working on this since', a date no earlier than the project's creation and no later than today; the date posted; an activity level in five steps (Very active, Active, Some work, Quiet, Dormant) counted from the weeks in the last 13 with real work by members, never the assistant's and never by volume, by a published method and re-signed monthly; 'Interested in collaborating' with a link to knock, only if the owner chooses; and links to any case the project published. Nothing else from the project's private work. Each counted week gets an independent timestamp on a sealed summary of its work, opened when the project publishes. An owner signs the notice as a case is signed, and it is published at the group's own public address, whose page lists its owners' public keys without names. Stopping says so, with an optional handoff note; a notice Dormant for a further month lapses. As holder of the domains, Bob set the network site's policy: the directory lives at believeincities.org/<place> (believein.city for short; believeinoakland.org redirects to /oakland), reads notices where groups publish them, records when it first saw each and keeps them all, and shows each as Stated (typed by hand; lapses after 60 days), Reported (signed by a group's copy) or Proven (its seals opened at publication). A group's record is facts, never a score; notices on the same matter sit side by side, each saying others are welcome; public pages never say 'claim'; impossible combinations are flagged; a group with no published work may have at most two open notices. The screens are Design's.

    30 The forms of a published case: the public page, the complete edition and the case file

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-82, DEC-112, DEC-119): The test of a published case is that anybody can review it, and even recreate it, without CivicOS. It comes in three forms. The public page: each finding opens with one line naming its role and the project's bar ('Relied on · meets this project's bar (capture B, connection C)'), never a bare 'meets', then its two grades, then the detail; the strength section opens 'a case has two strengths, never one'. The complete edition (what was called print): a self-contained file anyone can open with nothing to install, with no length limit, ordered so a reader can stop early, carrying every layer, the grading method and 'How to check this case yourself'; every page carries the identifying notice, and printing is incidental. The case file: the complete structured record in a published, versioned format rich enough that the results can be recreated, holding the complete edition, every document and observation a conclusion rests on (whole, with its extracted text), the findings' chains, attestations, signatures, and the method version inside the signed case; split into parts, never trimmed; an open specification and a standalone open checker let anyone recreate it. Everything a conclusion rests on must be presentable: publishing is refused while a relied-on finding rests on material that cannot travel whole; the warning says the case republishes these documents and that judging whether they may be is the group's. An off-the-record source's material travels whole with its attestations (member, project, group); only the identity is withheld, labelled 'Withheld' with its reason. Evidence from a member credited only at the group or project level counts as anonymous and cannot carry a relied-on finding without independent corroboration, including when that member attests an off-the-record source. Another group's copy imports a case file into a new, read-only project, and the system confirms each finding by recreating it: Recreated, Recreated in part (naming what is missing) or Did not recreate (naming what differs), shown also against the importing group's own bar. Recreating is not endorsing; the group may accept an edition only for recreated findings, and an accepted finding may support its own work, marked as another group's. The screens are Design's.

    31 The litigation hold: what it stops, how far it reaches, and what it blocks

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (K899 (7), K901, DEC-113, K1019): A hold is a member's recorded statement on a reply marked as a legal threat, 'hold in place' or 'hold released', always with a reason; the latest stands, and it is the reminder's only way out. Once devices store assistant transcripts, 'hold in place' stops both scheduled deletions (at the time limit and at publication), on every member's device, for the threatened action's project (filled in automatically) and any other projects the member names; more can be added by a further statement. A device checks for a hold before deleting and deletes nothing if it cannot check. Any member who can see the action may place a hold; releasing is heavier: its form says what will be deleted and that this cannot be undone, and the administrators and whoever placed the hold are told once. Members who can see a held project see a strip: 'Litigation hold in place since 2 Oct 2026, recorded by Paula Reyes. Assistant transcripts for this project stay on your device: they are not deleted after the usual time or at publication. Please do not clear them yourself.' Members who cannot see the project see nothing. While any hold is in place, the operator's wipe of the real record is refused, and the doorbell archive's one-week clearing pauses. Counsel's review of these defaults is advised.

    32 The everyday word for one item in the record

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (K899): Text members read says 'record' where it said 'bundle' (for example, 'This record was not found'); the internal identifiers stay as they are. Ruled by Bob through the development process on his secondary account, 1 October (K899 (1)).

    33 What the current app does until the redesign arrives

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (K633): Settled by your ruling of 30 September: 'You can leave the old interface, as it's not currently being worked on or tested, and will be replaced by the new interface once its design has evolved sufficiently.' It was recorded as: the old app stays as it is, untested and unworked, until the new interface replaces it, and no stage of the build plan maps or empties it before then. The same day you said no release is cut until you say so. The current and next build plans now list the old app's gaps (the six missing record types, the action form, the conflict and disclosure screens) as 'untouched until the new interface replaces it'. This is option B of the earlier brief, 'wait for the redesign'.

    34 What a plan is about: 'Matters'

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-114): Members see 'Matters' (one 'matter') for the things an action plan addresses, everywhere they appear: the plan page's heading and add button, the options' tags, the preview when an action starts, queue items and the glossary. The approved plan-page sketch changes in that one word. 'Subject' keeps its one member-facing meaning: a person in a public role, office, place, law or thing on the Subjects screen. The requirements' internal term stays 'subject'.

    35 Which Action sketches bind the redesign

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (K608 (4), DEC-115): Besides the plan page, the starting-and-sending sketch binds the redesign's content, step order and wording: the refusal shown before anything runs, the reason asked in place, and approving kept separate from recording the sending. In the third sheet, the tier 2 filing draft and the tier 3 counsel packet panels bind likewise. The matter page, the standards list and the queue items stay examples the designer may rework within the requirements and the later rulings (the queue's three kinds, the hold strip, 'Matters'). Look and layout stay Design's throughout, and where a bound sketch predates a later ruling ('Matters'; the filing-template rules), the ruling wins.

    36 After publication: a case's docket, outside responses, and withdrawing a case

    Settled by Bob, 2026-10-01 (DEC-100, DEC-116): Each case has a docket: dated entries, each naming the edition it concerns, growing while the signed editions never change. The named subject has party-like standing, and a group may grant the same standing to others by a signed entry with a reason (permanent for what they already submitted). There are no confidential entries; only the submitter redacts, and the group may decline redacted submissions. Three shelves: what the group stands behind as accurately listed; 'Reactions elsewhere' ('Listed by the group. Not evidence. Not endorsed.'); and 'In our record only', never published. Checks are on form, never merit, and an entry never makes anything evidence. Required on the first shelf, each a To-do for the project's manager: every response the subject sends; the subject's public statements the group has captured, a press release included; a newer edition with its 'What changed' statement; a withdrawal; anything that would have had to be disclosed at signing. Outside responses are found by a pasted link, a standing watch or a labelled suggestion; captured and co-archived; linked to an edition; and a member chooses, with a reason, record only, public or both. A public reaction shows date, source, links to the original and the archived copy, and a short summary in the group's words, never the whole article. A reply naming a private person is listed as received, without its text, and the subject is asked to resend it without the name. Any member files to the record; only the manager signs an entry into public view; an entry is taken back by a later one, never deleted. Withdrawal is the manager's signed, final entry naming an edition or all, with a published reason: the edition stays readable under a stamp, re-evaluation notices go to everything resting on it, it is never lifted, and nothing is erased. The docket travels by pull: the public page, a feed per case, citing groups' copies and the directory; a copy that cannot reach it says 'Could not read the publisher's docket'.

    Other rulings that shape what members see

    A project's stage and its cases' readiness

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (K362, K364): 'Closed' is the owner's recorded act with its judged reason; the other three stages are always computed. Each readiness rung shows by its own evidence; a published case reads Distributed, never Draft ('newspapers don't publish drafts'); Internally checked and Externally compliant read 'not yet evaluated' until the evaluations exist.

    build/rulings.md K362, K364

    What a member reads when an act is refused

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-83): A refused act shows the standard sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative, as part of what the member reads, never a hidden field; every refusal that shares a code follows this pattern.

    DECISIONS.md DEC-83; K446

    Thirteen points on presenting and resolving a contradiction

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-84): An undetermined candidate is a lead; a record duty binds the joined members of every project drawing on either side until resolved; two projects' different conclusions are plurality, each attributed, unless they cannot both hold in the same respect; the question's own conclusion resolves a contradiction inquiry; the machine recommends only which respects may differ; a stale side moves nothing by itself; a hypothesised cause is published as 'cause not established'; an irreconcilable conclusion is disclosed; publication discloses, never blocks; accepting keeps the machine's reason the machine's; 'one of them is wrong' needs a written reason; a dismissal gives one fixed reason.

    DECISIONS.md DEC-84; K450

    A conflict with a record the member cannot see

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (DEC-85): Each project in such a conflict is told of it on its own side only, where it sits in that project, never the other project, its members or its side. Each notice lets the project ask to resolve it; when every project holding a side has asked, the projects are named to each other's members, names only. Members then respond to the notices; a response is relayed with only what the responder chose to share, their cover or an email ('stated, not verified') if they wish, none of it required. There is no discussion thread and no general messaging. A conflict that remains at publication is told to the publisher in the ceremony and highlighted in the published case, which never names the hidden record or who holds it.

    DECISIONS.md DEC-85; K455, K456, K459; contradiction R49-R55

    Sources, self-registered keys and doorbell material

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (K509): A source's dated, attributed disclosure history has its own home, seen only by those allowed and every read logged. A member registers their own browser-held signing key, made on their own device and confirmed by passphrase or device unlock at each use; every administrator is told and can revoke it. Doorbell material is graded received, not fetched: its chain of custody is the knock's receipt. A pseudonym is an identity detail, so a case says 'an unnamed source' unless the source consented, by their knocker secret or through a member's evidenced record. Hand-carried material follows a recorded rule and is taken in with the upload redesign.

    build/rulings.md K509; sources R1-R15; membership R89-R91; provenance R51

    The Action layer's shape

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-29 (Rulings 1-10 (K590)): The Action layer has an action plan: a member creates it, the system suggests options, and the member chooses and lays them out; it is not project management. There is no catalogue of response options and no budgets. One plan may address several related matters, and one option may serve several. A plan may start from a compliant or unclear determination, or open as soon as an inquiry begins. Lobbying is an option only to enforce or restore an existing requirement. An unticked option is undecided; declining is an optional act with a reason. A plan holds up to three scenarios. Options fall into mitigation, legal, awareness, journalistic, grassroots or other.

    build/rulings.md K590 (rulings 1-10), K608 (1); docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §3, §4 rules 3, 4, 8, 9; build/plan/action-design/HANDOFF.md 'What Bob ruled'

    Who and what an action may address, and who drafts

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (D1-D6 (K590)): An action is addressed to a government office by role and body, a reporter or outlet, an organisation or another civic group by role and organisation, or a described audience; never a private individual, and a breach action is addressed to an office (D1). The assistant drafts communications as proposals a member adopts (D2). An action may resolve as 'completed' when no counterparty's answer decides it, its own outcome saying what happened (D3). A project may declare its kind of work (reporting, fixing, legal, oversight, other), which shapes the assistant's suggestions and nothing else (D4). A started option may name the group's contact, which grants nothing (D5). Any group may use CivicOS; a group with a stake in a matter discloses it (D6).

    build/rulings.md K590 (D1-D6), K608 (1); docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §4 rules 6, 9, 10; actions R7, R9, R45; action-plans R21; filings R23

    A plan closes only by a member, with a reason

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (action-plans R20 (K590)): A member closes a plan with a reason; a plan never closes itself. A closed plan stays readable, and its subjects are free to join another plan.

    action-plans R20; build/rulings.md K590; build/plan/action-design/HANDOFF.md 'What Bob ruled'

    Acting on an unestablished premise

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (Action §7 point 2 (K600, K608)): An action that asserts a breach is refused by default unless it rests on a live noncompliant determination. A member may proceed anyway, openly, by an attributed act with a stated reason; the action and every filing, counsel packet and communication prepared from it then say first that it rests on an unestablished premise, with the reason, who and when. An action that seeks evidence, such as a records request or a request for comment, is never gated, and an overridden action never joins an escalation. A plan may rest on premises not yet established, shown as hunch debt.

    docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §4 rule 2, §7 point 2, §8; build/rulings.md K600 (a), K608 (1); actions R8; filings R24; escalation R23

    The venue sets the standard of evidence

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (Action §4 rule 13 (K597, K608)): No action is refused for its evidence grade. Every filing and counsel packet shows each exhibit's grade and whether it is co-attested; where the jurisdiction profile states a venue's standard of evidence, it is shown beside them, and a grade the opposition could contest is flagged so counsel and members can prepare. Grade A is the highest grade the product offers: a ceiling, not a minimum.

    docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §4 rule 13, §7 point 3; build/rulings.md K597 (3), K600 (b), K608 (1); filings R25; jurisdictions R39

    Good faith hoped for, opposition prepared for

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (Action §4 rule 12 (K597, K608)): People are presumed to want better outcomes, and a bad actor is identified by evidence, never by role. The system is nonetheless prepared for opposition: every plan is checked for a branch that answers a hostile response; pressure against the group or its supporters is recorded as evidence and can open an inquiry; and what counsel or another group needs to carry a matter on survives the group's disruption.

    docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §4 rule 12, §7 point 1; build/rulings.md K597 (1), K608 (1); action-plans R19; actions R48

    Deferred: licensed certification and joint action with other groups

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (Action §7 points 4 and 6 (K597, K600, K608)): Certification of an output by a licensed professional is deferred; it comes back when a group needs a licensed name on an output, and meanwhile the attribution levels serve. Joint action with another group is recorded and deferred, including the near-term support described for it; it comes back when a coalition of groups asks to act jointly.

    docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §7 points 4 and 6, §8; build/rulings.md K597 (4), K600 (c), K608 (1)

    Confidential referral

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (Action §7 point 5 (K597, K608)): A confidential referral is an action addressed to the oversight office, prepared as a filing or communication, sent by the member's own hand and recorded; nothing non-public leaves by a system path.

    docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md §7 point 5; build/rulings.md K597 (5), K608 (1); DEC-31

    The old interface, and when a release is cut

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (K633): The old interface (civicos-ui/app.html) stays as it is, untested and unworked, until the new interface replaces it: in Bob's words, "You can leave the old interface, as it's not currently being worked on or tested, and will be replaced by the new interface once its design has evolved sufficiently". No release is cut until he says so ("releases aren't being cut until I say that we're ready to do one"), so what is built reaches a group's copy only then.

    build/rulings.md K633 (Bob, in BOB #75)

    The assistant's planning suggestions

    Settled by Bob, 2026-09-30 (K660): Only a joined member starts a planning run, one plan at a time, never automatically. "Rather than a hard limit of 5 suggestions, the feature should present the best first 5. But also provide a means for the member to ask to see the next 'tranche' of 5 options, and so on": the tray shows the five strongest first and the member asks for the next five until none remain, with no score shown. The run drafts no letters, reads only the same project's earlier plans, and may plan for suspected matters, flagged by the plan's checks. The machine-work disclosure stops at adoption: the adopted option keeps it, the action and what is sent do not, and the record keeps who adopted it, from which run and when. It is switched on as soon as development can properly support it ("No legit reason I can think of not to switch it on as soon as development can properly support it").

    build/rulings.md K660 (Bob, in BOB #75); action-plans R30-R34; agent-worker R50-R53; run-rules R13-R14; skills R29

    Part 2 · Design inputs

    Who meets it

    Everyone who uses CivicOS or reads what it produces, inside and outside the group: what they want, what they already know, the conditions they meet it in, and what they must never see or be able to do.

    Group operator (installs the copy)

    • The person who puts the group's own copy of CivicOS into the group's own cloud account, from a page opened in a browser. May or may not become the founder. Fixed installer Purpose, R1; Distribution section 2 (a sovereign instance, not a hosted service)

    Goals

    • Get a working copy running in the group's account, proven to serve the signed release, and later update it without touching the group's record or credentials. Fixed installer Purpose, R15, R17-R18, R33
    • Choose the jurisdiction profiles the copy reads its local facts from. Fixed installer R21; instance-setup R13

    Already knows

    • Must already hold a paid cloud account with a payment method; the pages state these prerequisites. Fixed installer R7, R23
    • How much technical knowledge can the install page assume? The canon says the audience overall is non-technical but does not say the operator is. Open

    Context

    • Device:
      • A browser; the installer is a web page. Fixed installer R1
    • TimePressure:
      • A scoped, short-lived permission is granted for the install and never stored. Fixed installer Purpose
    • Frequency:
      • Once to install; again on each update. Fixed installer R17-R18
    • Stakes:
      • The final panel shows the one-time password and the member and probe credentials ONCE; losing them matters. Fixed installer R16
      • The one-time password becomes the root of trust, whose custody is not modelled (deferred). Fixed DEC-2 (deferred); Membership v2 section 9

    Trust concerns

    • Needs proof, not a claim, that each part serves the signed release before the page says so. Fixed installer Purpose, R15, R33
    • Believe in Oakland appears only as publisher and signer of the release; every page names CivicOS and the installing group. Fixed installer R22
    • Installing into an account that already holds a copy is refused until installs are isolated. Fixed installer R24, R32

    Must never see or do

    • Never sees the cloud access token or the daemon credential on any page, log or error. Fixed installer R16, R19
    • Never has an existing copy silently overwritten. Fixed installer R5, R32

    Open installer; instance-setup; Distribution v0.1

    Founder (root of trust)

    • The first person to claim the copy with its one-time password. Holds everything an administrator holds and cannot be voted out by administrators; the hosting account is the remedy. Fixed membership R1, R7; inventory roles

    Goals

    • Claim the copy, name the group, add a second administrator, start inviting. Fixed inventory journey (a); membership R1, R11-R13
    • Can deliver a signed case from their own session (today's only live publishing route). Fixed AI Roles section 3 rule 4 (D-421); Publication section 5

    Already knows

    • Holds the one-time password handed over by the operator (or is the operator). Fixed installer R16; instance-setup R21 (#boot= pre-fills it)

    Context

    • Device:
      • The setup page is served at the copy's root; the canon does not say which device a founder uses. Open
    • TimePressure:
      • Until claimed, the copy is unclaimed and anyone with the password could claim it. Fixed membership R1; inventory group-instance states
    • Frequency:
      • Claim once; set identity rarely. Fixed instance-setup R4, R26
    • Stakes:
      • The group slug is written once and can never change; a group wanting to stay unnamed must pick an unrevealing slug at install. Fixed Publication section 7 point 1; instance-setup R26
      • Full working-corpus export requires this credential and notifies every administrator. Fixed Membership v2 section 8.1

    Trust concerns

    • A founder-delivered ratification is recorded as founder-delivered, beside the signer. Fixed AI Roles section 3 rule 4; publication R14, R27
    • DEC-2 is deferred: how a founder learns what losing or leaking the root credential costs is not designed. Open

    Must never see or do

    • Cannot act inside a project without participating. Fixed inventory roles; membership R60

    Open membership; instance-setup; DEC-2

    Administrator (custodian)

    • A member who keeps the roster, capabilities, keys and group identity. A custodian of shared infrastructure, not an authority over the network's content or work. Fixed Design Requirement 1; Membership v2 sections 4-5

    Goals

    • Invite members with covers and capabilities, keep at least two administrators, confirm expertise, register signing keys, set the group bar, instance bias, group aspirations, profiles and identity. Fixed membership R5-R14, R21-R27; strength R15; bias R11; intent R9; instance-setup R5-R15

    Already knows

    • Sees cover and handle together (only administrators do). Fixed Membership v2 section 3
    • Sees every project and participant list, but sight is never a position in a project. Fixed membership R43, R60

    Context

    • Device:
      • Not stated. Open
    • TimePressure:
      • A ballot to add or remove an administrator waits for other administrators; arithmetic is computed by the plane. Fixed membership R5-R8
    • Frequency:
      • Not stated; the canon implies occasional custodial work. Open
    • Stakes:
      • Cover is required and must not invite a legal name; a group under pressure should use covers that do not resolve to civil identities. Fixed Membership v2 section 3
      • At two administrators, removal is impossible; resignation is refused at the floor. Fixed membership R7, R10

    Trust concerns

    • Refusals must be comprehensible: the two-administrator floor and ballot arithmetic are shown with the denominator. Fixed UI-KICKOFF 'Who this is for' item 4; Interaction Constructs section B
    • Membership is not a security boundary: interfaces must not imply project privacy is secrecy. Fixed Membership v2 section 2

    Must never see or do

    • Cannot publish, ratify, set visibility or answer join requests for a project as administrator; the only project act is rescuing an ownerless project. Fixed membership R41, R45, R51, R60; case-authoring R2
    • A capability they do not hold is absent from the interface, not greyed. Fixed Membership v2 section 5; UI-KICKOFF constraints

    Fixed membership; Membership v2

    Member, newcomer (no civic engagement experience)

    • A community activist, not a technologist; the interface's centre of gravity. Fixed UI-KICKOFF 'Who this is for' item 1
    • The system must be useful to one person with a few hours a week; materials must be understandable by someone with no prior civic engagement experience and usable within one session. Fixed Design Requirements 2 and 11

    Goals

    • Follow the path: questioning, exploring, discovering, documenting, impacting. Fixed System Design section 1; Case Making 'The frame'

    Already knows

    • May know nothing about grades, strength, rungs or UNDETERMINED; load-bearing state must be named so a newcomer can recognise it and learn its implications. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (load-bearing complexity is per-level)
    • A newcomer meets two things first: strength against the project's bar, and 'Undetermined'; every other mark is taught where it is used, its explanation one hover away. Fixed DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §M
    • The onboarding path and starter materials are deferred until the redesigned surfaces are built and stable. Fixed DEC-91

    Context

    • Device:
      • Phones are supported, acceptably as a viewing MVP; features may be absent on some devices. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (devices)
    • TimePressure:
      • Statutory clocks run whether or not the member is ready (Operational Principle 3). Fixed Roadmap section 5
    • Frequency:
      • A few hours a week is the design point. Fixed Design Requirement 2
    • Stakes:
      • Personal exposure: communications with officials may be public records; opposition may target participants. Fixed Design Requirement 11 (security practices guide)

    Trust concerns

    • The workflow must not nag or second-guess the member; inform once at the act. Fixed DEC-69; System Design section 7
    • The system never puts words in a member's mouth: no prefilled or suggested reasons. Fixed Interaction Constructs section J; AI Roles section 3 rule 1

    Must never see or do

    • Never shown an act they cannot perform (capability absent). Fixed Membership v2 section 5
    • Never shown working material they may not see; a hidden project answers as if it does not exist. Fixed membership R61; case-authoring R27
    • Never shown UNDETERMINED as an error. Fixed Interaction Constructs §U; DEC-86

    Open UI-KICKOFF; Design Requirements 2, 11; DEC-69

    Member, experienced investigator

    • A member who works questions, cites, groups support into sets of reasons, runs checks and prepares cases. Fixed UI-KICKOFF 'Who this is for' items 1-3; inventory roles (member with contribute)

    Goals

    • Build a supported case easily; find it hard to state an unsupported one. Fixed Case Making section 4a
    • Follow hunches and plan from what is not yet established, with the gate at the act, not at the reasoning. Fixed Case Making section 6a; Declared Bias (DEC-15)

    Already knows

    • Knows the domain; still must see relevant load-bearing state at every level. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements

    Context

    • TimePressure:
      • Deadlines are deadlines. Fixed Operational Principle 3
    • Frequency:
      • Not stated. Open
    • Stakes:

    Trust concerns

    • Wants the pre-flight to be the plane's own answer, never a surface's guess. Fixed Interaction Constructs RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-8)
    • Wants machine work visibly labelled as machine work. Fixed AI Roles section 3 rule 3

    Must never see or do

    • Never shown AND/OR, 'grounds', partition, branch or 'independently sufficient' when grouping support into sets of reasons. Fixed affordances R5 (DEC-32 clause 1); strength R28

    Fixed Case Making; Interaction Constructs; strength

    Member with declared professional expertise (lawyer, CPA, engineer)

    • A member who declares expertise; an administrator may confirm it. Confirmation gates nothing. Fixed Membership v2 section 1.3; membership R21-R24

    Goals

      • Not stated. Open

      • Not stated. Open
    • Frequency:
      • Not stated. Open
    • Stakes:

    Trust concerns

    Knocker (anyone handing the group material)

      • Rare. Fixed Intake Doctrine section 2a
    • Stakes:
      • The source is counted by a keyed fingerprint of the connecting address, never the address. Fixed capture R31, R56

    Trust concerns

    • The record names them as an unnamed knocker identified by the knock's receipt, never as 'unknown'; the member who pulls the material is never recorded as its source. Fixed DEC-78 item 2; Intake Doctrine §2a
    • Their note travels with the material as their own words, never as evidence of its truth; their contact stays in the doorbell inbox and never enters the record or any publication. Fixed DEC-78 item 4
    • The group is never the first to make them more public: an identity detail is published only with their consent to that audience, or where it is already public, cited. Fixed DEC-78 item 5(a)
    • What the knocker is told about what happens next, beyond the receipt and the optional secret, is not designed. Open
