      The experience, step by step

      The long paths through the work, as the person taking each step meets it: what they know at that moment, what they decide, what can go wrong, how long it takes, who they hand it to, and where trust is at risk.

      (a) A group sets up and invites members · 10 steps
      1. group operator · One sitting; up to ten self-test tries.
        Install the signed release into the group's account
        Knows
        Needs a paid account with a payment method; the page states prerequisites.
        Decides
        Which slug to use (written once, public forever) and which jurisdiction profiles to offer.
        Can go wrong
        • Existing copy for the slug: refused, nothing changed (installer R5).
        • No payment method: refused, saying nothing was created (installer R4, R7).
        • Account already holds a copy: refused until installs are isolated (installer R32).
        • A part not serving the release: named, not claimed as done (installer R15, R33).
        Hands to
        founder (the one-time password, shown once)
        Trust risk
        The slug is public and permanent; a group wanting anonymity must choose an unrevealing one now.
        Open here
        • How the operator is told, in plain words, that the slug is permanent and public at the moment of choosing.

        Open installer R2-R16, R21-R24, R32-R33; Publication section 7 point 1

      2. founder · Minutes.
        Open the setup page and claim with the one-time password
        Knows
        Holds the password (the link may carry it as #boot=).
        Decides
        Choose a password of at least 12 characters.
        Can go wrong
        • ALREADY_CLAIMED (membership R1).
        • PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT.
        • The page could not read the group: says so, never 'none recorded' (instance-setup R20).
        Hands to
        founder
        Trust risk
        The founder becomes the root of trust with no custody model (DEC-2 deferred).

        Fixed membership R1; instance-setup R20-R22; control-plane R15

      3. founder / administrator · Minutes; domain re-checked daily.
        Set display name, domain and jurisdiction profiles
        Knows
        The slug; the held profiles by name and coverage.
        Decides
        What the group calls itself; which domain to claim; which profiles (none preselected).
        Can go wrong
        • GROUP_DISPLAY_NAME_MALFORMED.
        • Domain verdict absent, mismatched or UNDETERMINED: not shown publicly; members see the state (instance-setup R8-R10).
        • Changing profiles changes how local facts read (warning; instance-setup R15).
        Hands to
        administrators
        Trust risk
        Showing an unverified domain would claim an association the record cannot support.

        Fixed instance-setup R5-R16; Publication section 7 (profiles built T12, N10)

      4. founder · Minutes; then waits for the invitee.
        Add a second administrator (direct while fewer than two)
        Knows
        The floor is two administrators.
        Decides
        Who, with what cover (not a legal name).
        Can go wrong
        • NOT_AN_ADMIN.
        • Cover typed as a legal name defeats the protection (Membership v2 section 3).
        Hands to
        the invitee
        Trust risk
        Cover invites a legal name if the field is worded badly.

        Fixed membership R12-R14; Membership v2 section 3

      5. the group, asked when the second administrator is added · Minutes.
        Record who holds hosting access
        Knows
        Hosting access is outside the app.
        Decides
        Whom to record.
        Can go wrong
        • Left unanswered: the record keeps whatever answer was given.
        Hands to
        administrators
        Trust risk
        None named in canon.
        Open here
        • What the group is told about why this matters (the hosting account is the remedy against the founder).

        Open membership R11

      6. administrator · Minutes per member.
        Add members with capabilities (default contribute)
        Knows
        Capabilities: contribute, publish, create_projects.
        Decides
        Cover, capabilities, declared expertise.
        Can go wrong
        • At two or more administrators, a new administrator is only proposed and needs a ballot (membership R14).
        • BAD_CAPABILITY.
        Hands to
        invitee (burner URL sent by a channel the system does not record)
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed membership R9, R12-R15; Membership v2 section 6

      7. invitee · Minutes; the link is spent on use.
        Open the link, choose handle and password
        Knows
        Sees cover, role, capabilities and expertise; never the member id.
        Decides
        Handle (public in the record) and password.
        Can go wrong
        • NO_SUCH_INVITATION (spent or never existed, one answer).
        • HANDLE_TAKEN.
        • BAD_HANDLE.
        • PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT.
        • ENROL_NOT_RECORDED: the enrolment could not be completed, nothing changed, and the invitation link still works (membership R95; T19: K778).
        Hands to
        member (active)
        Trust risk
        The handle is what the public sees; the member may not realise.
        Open here
        • What a newcomer learns first is deferred until the redesign is built and stable (DEC-91); meanwhile strength against the bar and 'Undetermined' come first (DEC-82).

        Open membership R15-R16; Membership v2 section 3; membership R95 (K778)

      8. member with create_projects · Minutes.
        Create a project with an objective
        Knows
        A project name is unique across the instance.
        Decides
        Name and objective.
        Can go wrong
        • NAME_TAKEN.
        • A project with no objective is refused (intent R1).
        Hands to
        owner
        Trust risk
        Members may believe projects are private; visibility is organisation, not secrecy.

        Fixed promotion R19; intent R1; membership R71; Membership v2 section 2

      9. owner, then participant · Waits for each invitee.
        Invite participants; they join
        Knows
        Handles.
        Decides
        Whom to invite.
        Can go wrong
        • NOT_THE_OWNER.
        • NO_SUCH_HANDLE.
        • ALREADY_A_PARTICIPANT.
        • NOT_INVITED on join.
        Hands to
        participants
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed membership R32-R34

      10. owner, outside member · Days; requests can lapse.
        Make the project discoverable; an outsider requests to join; owner grants
        Knows
        Outsiders see the name only.
        Decides
        Whether to expose the name; whether to grant.
        Can go wrong
        • PROJECT_SEEN_NOT_A_PARTICIPANT (C-70.1) for acts beyond existence.
        • A grant invites; the requester still has to join (membership R51).
        Hands to
        owner
        Trust risk
        Exposing a name tells every member the project exists.

        Fixed membership R44-R53, R77

      (b) From a question to a published case · 16 steps
      1. member · Seconds to minutes; renders may be deferred.
        Capture a public document
        Knows
        An address.
        Decides
        Which route (direct, rendered, archive, Drive).
        Can go wrong
        • BAD_LOCATOR.
        • HOST_COOLING_OFF: the governor paces the host (capture R7).
        • Source refused or fetch failed, recorded as such (capture R8).
        • Rendered content UNDETERMINED; archive capture at most C.
        Hands to
        member (release)
        Trust risk
        A capture is not evidence until a member stands behind it; the grade note may be discarded before the member sees it (DEC-51).

        Fixed capture R2-R18; AI Roles rule 4; DEC-51

      2. member at the release moment · Minutes per batch.
        Release it (vouch for it)
        Knows
        Must see the source material itself, never only an AI summary.
        Decides
        Stand behind it, with acknowledgement and mitigation for a bulk release.
        Can go wrong
        • Refused whole, with offenders named (weight refuse: all or nothing): not Information, not collected, crucial (read from the document's own front matter too), or missing an entry requirement (ratification R22-R23; N425).
        • A machine, or an empty or over-long acknowledgment or mitigation, is refused before anything is read (MACHINE_CANNOT_RELEASE, NO_ACKNOWLEDGMENT, NO_MITIGATION; ratification R20).
        • A second member released first: CAS_STALE (promotion R1).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Click-through bulk release would make the acknowledgement dishonest.
        Open here
        • A failure partway leaves the documents already released released, and names them (ratification R25): how the screen shows that is the redesign's.

        Open UI-KICKOFF item 2; Interaction Constructs section J; affordances R1-R2; ratification R20-R27 (K692; N425, K792)

      3. member · Minutes.
        Resolve its references to subjects
        Knows
        Recognised references with grades.
        Decides
        Accept matches; testify a missed one (grade D).
        Can go wrong
        • Ambiguous alias kept, never resolved silently (entities R6).
        • A C match never reads established.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        One click from an established resolution off a machine-composed list (DEC-53).

        Fixed entities R9-R12, R27; DEC-53

      4. member or assistant · Minutes; ageing interval set per instance.
        Open a question (or the assistant surfaces one)
        Knows
        What they want to know.
        Decides
        Wording of the question.
        Can go wrong
        • Surfaced by the assistant: must look surfaced; ages to deferred if nobody acts (intent R17).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        A machine question must not read as a member's obligation (D-82).

        Fixed inquiry R1-R2; ai-runs R25-R27; intent R16-R17

      5. member · Ongoing, days.
        Cite documents, passages or questions as supporting or cutting against
        Knows
        Earned grades per target.
        Decides
        Role of each leg.
        Can go wrong
        • RETIRED_NOT_CITABLE.
        • A grouped question refuses a new leg until ungrouped (DEC-50).
        • A newer capture may exist: stale version notice.
        • A supporting and a cutting-against leg that conflict carry a tension mark; the clarifier asks how they differ (decided: DEC-77).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Stating a grade the record did not earn: the leg is capped and why is shown.

        Fixed citation R1-R8; inquiry R4-R14; DEC-50

      6. member · Minutes to hours; resumable.
        Open an assistant run to check or investigate
        Knows
        The run's bounds, lens and bar are recorded at open.
        Decides
        Mode (only CHECK deployed).
        Can go wrong
        • Mode not deployed: refused (ai-runs R40).
        • A bound stops the run and the log says which.
        • Capture requests wait on the daemon (hours).
        Hands to
        assistant, then member
        Trust risk
        The assistant appearing to conclude; transcripts are notes, never record.

        Fixed ai-runs R9-R24, R40; AI Roles section 3

      7. member · Minutes.
        Accept a version (after the shared-origin check)
        Knows
        Versions with legs and per-axis strength; suggested ones labelled.
        Decides
        Accept, reject, consider, revert; preview first.
        Can go wrong
        • VERSION_* refusals; MACHINE_CANNOT_MOVE_VERSION for a machine.
        • Grounds that share an origin.
        Hands to
        project participant
        Trust risk
        Accepting a machine suggestion without reading it (DEC-68: the record cannot tell).

        Fixed basis-versions R4-R14; strength R11-R12; DEC-68

      8. joined participant · Minutes.
        Make it current for the project
        Knows
        Which version is accepted.
        Decides
        Which account the project stands on.
        Can go wrong
        • VERSION_NOT_ACCEPTED.
        • Not a joined participant.
        Hands to
        joined participant
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed basis-versions R13, R15, R31

      9. joined participant · Minutes.
        Conclude, stating what would falsify it (or that nothing would)
        Knows
        The current version and its strength.
        Decides
        The conclusion and falsifier.
        Can go wrong
        • MACHINE_CANNOT_CONCLUDE.
        • NO_CLAIM.
        • Another project concluded differently: both stances stand.
        Hands to
        owner
        Trust risk
        False certainty: a falsifier of 'none' must be the member's stated override.

        Fixed basis-versions R16-R19; System Design row 8 (UI-64)

      10. owner · Days if more evidence is needed.
        Check strength against the bar; clear hunches
        Knows
        Per-axis pair per load-bearing finding; the project bar.
        Decides
        Which findings are load-bearing; which hunches to clear.
        Can go wrong
        • BELOW_PROJECT_STRENGTH naming member, axis, required and reached (case-authoring R6).
        • UNCLEARED_HUNCH (not yet enforced; lands with the ceremony's pre-flight, DEC-80 item 3).
        Hands to
        owner
        Trust risk
        Over-claiming: a case over an uncleared hunch states a strength that is not true.

        Fixed case-authoring R6, R12; strength R5; DEC-20

      11. editor / owner · Days (waiting on reviewers).
        Draft a review copy; grant outside reviewers
        Knows
        The copy is complete as a publication can be and names what is missing.
        Decides
        Whom to grant; when to revoke.
        Can go wrong
        • Grant dies if the draft moves case identity (review R8).
        • Recipient sees findings as the last editor could, absent ones by role only.
        Hands to
        review recipient
        Trust risk
        Calling it 'pre-publish' asserts a future that may not happen.

        Fixed review R3-R17; Publication section 6A

      12. owner · Hours.
        Prepare the case: scope, statement, exclusions, bias acknowledgement
        Knows
        Concluded findings; the searched section computed from the log.
        Decides
        Roles, scope, what is left out and why.
        Can go wrong
        • COMPLETENESS_CARRIED_FORWARD (byte-identical to last edition).
        • CASE_SEARCHED_UNCOMPUTABLE.
        • ALREADY_A_CASE_MEMBER.
        • CASE_IDENTITY_AMBIGUOUS.
        Hands to
        members / reviewers
        Trust risk
        Nothing may draft framing for the member (Case Making section 4a).

        Fixed case-authoring R1-R18

      13. joined participant or review recipient (not its writer) · Days; optional.
        Acknowledge the exclusion statement
        Knows
        The statement, shown first.
        Decides
        Acknowledge, or not.
        Can go wrong
        • STATEMENT_ACK_BY_ITS_AUTHOR.
        • STATEMENT_ACK_NOT_A_PARTICIPANT.
        • STATEMENT_ACK_ALREADY_SIGNED.
        • STATEMENT_ACK_AUTHOR_UNDETERMINED.
        Hands to
        owner
        Trust risk
        A gate pushing a one-person group to recruit a signature would be a bug; nobody-but-author is disclosed, not refused.

        Fixed case-authoring R19-R21; Publication section 3 rule 11

      14. owner · Minutes, once the pre-flight is clear.
        Go through the publication ceremony and sign in the browser
        Knows
        Before the first screen, what would stop publication (the pre-flight). Then: what becomes permanent; what it rests on; what it leaves out, including any unresolved record contradiction to disclose and consent for any source identity it would reveal; the edition it creates (DEC-80 item 2).
        Decides
        Disclose, acknowledge, and sign now or not.
        Can go wrong
        • NO_SIGNERS.
        • CASE_RATIFY_STALE (an acknowledgement or edit re-authored it).
        • ATTRIBUTION_UNCHOSEN / ATTRIBUTION_STATEMENT_STALE.
        • OPERATOR_TOKEN_CANNOT_RATIFY_CASE.
        • CASE_CONCLUSION_MOVED.
        • An undisclosed open record tension refuses the case; a disclosed one does not (DEC-84 (13)).
        • A load-bearing document whose co-attestation failed: retried, then acknowledged with a reason and marked 'self-attested only' (DEC-81 item 3).
        • A finding rests on a side in conflict with a record the owner cannot see: told in the ceremony, highlighted in the case, never naming that record (DEC-85).
        Hands to
        signer with case authority
        Trust risk
        A member who thinks they certify authenticity has been misled (Interaction Constructs section A).
        Open here
        • Built at the plane (the pre-flight, the tensions read, the late retry, capture accounts, self-registered keys); the five screens and signing in the browser are not built; the words of each step are the redesign's.

        Open ratification R2-R3; DEC-80; DEC-81; DEC-76 item 4; DEC-78 item 5; case-authoring R31-R37; ratification R18-R19

      15. signer with case authority · Minutes.
        Ratify each finding and its evidence
        Knows
        Pinned members awaiting signature.
        Decides
        Sign each.
        Can go wrong
        • RATIFY_FINDING_NOT_IN_A_RATIFIED_CASE.
        • RATIFY_NOT_EVIDENCE_OF_A_RATIFIED_CASE.
        • A finding ratified before its evidence does not link to it on the published graph (Publication section 3 rule 2 gap).
        Hands to
        anyone (public)
        Trust risk
        Irreversible: correction only by a new edition.

        Fixed ratification R4-R6; Publication section 3 rules 1-2

      16. anyone · Any time.
        Read and verify by hash
        Knows
        No account.
        Decides
        Trust or not.
        Can go wrong
        • Not published: stated.
        • A finding pinned by several cases: the reader chooses (FINDING_IN_SEVERAL_CASES).
        • Cases disagree on a finding's pair: CASES_DISAGREE, each case's pair shown.
        Hands to
        none
        Trust risk
        Reading strength as a single score, or authenticity into a hash.

        Fixed publication R8-R13, R26-R28

      (c) Acting on a finding · 13 steps
      1. member · Minutes.
        Declare the standard (or adopt a proposal)
        Knows
        The standard's captured text; profile source match.
        Decides

        Can go wrong

        • Source undetermined when the profile does not supply it.
        Hands to
        joined member
        Trust risk
        None named.

      2. joined member · Hours.
        Record a determination per standard
        Knows
        Published findings with frozen pairs; standards in force.
        Decides
        Compliant, noncompliant or unclear, per standard.
        Can go wrong
        • A standard not in force at the act's date: refused.
        • Any significance, rank or score key: refused (R8).
        • basis_changed later when a finding reopens.
        Hands to
        joined member
        Trust risk
        Judging significance through policy preference (Functional Architecture Function 4).

        Fixed conformance R1-R10; Operational Principle 1

      3. joined member · Hours.
        Record consequences
        Knows
        What the record can compute; what must be assessed.
        Decides
        Computed, assessed or undetermined; causation.
        Can go wrong
        • NOT_NONCOMPLIANT.
        • A part naming an individual: refused.
        • Totals only within one state.
        Hands to
        joined member
        Trust risk
        Composing harm into one headline figure (refused).

        Fixed consequences R1-R12

      4. joined member · Minutes.
        Open an escalation
        Knows
        The determination is live and noncompliant.
        Decides
        Whether to pursue, and why, in their own words (decided: DEC-89).
        Can go wrong
        • MACHINE_CANNOT_OPEN.
        • DETERMINATION_SUPERSEDED.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed escalation R1; DEC-89

      5. joined member · Minutes.
        Or decline to escalate, saying why not now
        Knows
        The determination is live and noncompliant.
        Decides
        Why the group is not pursuing it now, in their own words.
        Can go wrong
        • Nothing may be recorded as significance, severity, priority, urgency or rank (conformance R8, escalation R19).
        Hands to
        the group; a later escalation, opened with its own reason, supersedes it and both stay readable
        Trust risk
        Until it is built, deciding not to escalate leaves no trace.
        Open here
        • Decided, not built: escalation, conformance and affordances requirements owed.

        Open DEC-89

      6. member · Minutes.
        Create a breach action to the office; set the risk tier
        Knows
        Counterparty by role and body from the profile.
        Decides
        Tier 1, 2 or 3 with a reason; governing laws.
        Can go wrong
        • Tier unset reads UNDETERMINED, never 1 (D-182).
        • MACHINE_CANNOT_SET_RISK_TIER.
        • LEASE_HELD (another member acting on the action).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Legally unsophisticated filing creating adverse precedent (Tier 3).

        Fixed actions R1-R24; Design Requirement 8

      7. member (machine may prepare) · Hours to send; then the clock.
        Prepare, approve and record the filing sent
        Knows
        Draft with [UNFILLED] blanks; Tier 2 advisory first.
        Decides
        Approve text (edited or not); send outside the system; record sent.
        Can go wrong
        • FILING_STALE (tier, counterparty, determination or laws changed).
        • STILL_UNFILLED.
        • NOT_TIER3 / Tier 3 yields no template.
        Hands to
        the office (outside)
        Trust risk
        A machine-prepared filing mistaken for the group's approved words.

      8. member · Statutory period (e.g. days).
        State the deadline with its legal basis
        Knows
        Profile deadlines, holidays.
        Decides
        The clock entry.
        Can go wrong
        • Every date carries its statute; overdue is derived, never silently stale (actions R35).
        Hands to
        plane (monitoring)
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed actions R32, R35; escalation R6

      9. plane · Runs on its own.
        The plane marks overdue and proposes the next stage
        Knows
        Clock dates; responses.
        Decides
        None: it proposes, never advances.
        Can go wrong
        • Not yet built: overdue marks and escalation prompts (monitoring R34-R35).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        The system appearing to decide the next stage.

        Fixed monitoring R34-R35, R44; escalation R2, R16-R17

      10. member · Days to weeks of waiting.
        Evaluate the response
        Knows
        Ledger entries; captured response.
        Decides
        complied / partial / denied / none, with reason.
        Can go wrong
        • Response arrives outside the system and must be captured.
        • Partial compliance does not stop the clock.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed escalation R10; Functional Architecture Function 5

      11. member · Minutes; proposals age visibly.
        Advance to legal tools or political accountability
        Knows
        Proposed edges with triggers.
        Decides
        Advance, decline with reason, suspend.
        Can go wrong
        • TRIGGER_NOT_MET naming what is missing.
        • ILLEGAL_STAGE.
        • NOT_OPEN.
        Hands to
        counsel or officials
        Trust risk
        Advocacy creeping in: stage 7 only enforces an existing requirement.

        Fixed escalation R8, R12-R16, R19

      12. member · Hours.
        Assemble a counsel packet (Tier 3)
        Knows
        Six sections from the record.
        Decides

        Can go wrong

        • basis_changed when a finding moves.
        Hands to
        named counsel
        Trust risk
        A packet read as legal advice or as fileable.

      13. member · Months.
        Record consequences addressed; end the escalation
        Knows

        Decides
        End.
        Can go wrong

        • CONSEQUENCES_NOT_ADDRESSED.
        • CONSEQUENCES_UNDETERMINED.
        Hands to
        none

      (d) The daily queue · 9 steps
      1. member · Minutes a day.
        Open the queue

      7. joined member · Minutes; days if taken up.
        Resolve a tension in our own record, or dismiss a lead
        Knows
        Both sides in the record's own words, with source and date and why they were paired; facts the record holds filled in with their source; the machine's recommendation, labelled.
        Decides
        How the two differ, which one is wrong (with a reason), or take it up as a contradiction inquiry; for a lead, dismiss with one fixed reason.
        Can go wrong
        • A record duty cannot be muted, dismissed or set aside (DEC-84 (2)).
        • Explained without evidence: the mark softens and does not clear (DEC-77 item 1).
        Hands to
        the contradiction inquiry, for 'they really conflict' or 'not sure'
        Trust risk
        Recommendations becoming decisions: the acceptance rate is measured and reviewed (DEC-77 item 3).
        Open here
        • Built at the plane; no member surface yet. No machine recommendation is produced live until the recommender prompt is measured (R41; K491).

      8. recipient · Minutes.

        Knows

        Decides

        Can go wrong

        Hands to
        none

      (f) From a knock to the record · 3 steps
      1. knocker (no account) · Seconds.
        Someone knocks with material
        Knows
        Nothing about the group.
        Decides
        What to send; an optional note and contact.
        Can go wrong
        • KNOCK_EMPTY, KNOCK_PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE, KNOCK_ENVELOPE_TOO_LARGE.
        • RATE_IP / RATE_GLOBAL (published bound; no member-facing translation yet, D-508).
        Hands to
        member (doorbell inbox)
        Trust risk
        A 429 is a claim that needs a count behind it.
        Open here
        • What the knocker is told about what happens next, beyond the receipt and the optional knocker secret (DEC-78 item 3).

        Open capture R30-R32, R47-R56; Intake Doctrine section 2a

      2. member · Days.
        A member reads the inbox and resolves the knock
        Knows
        The material, its digest, the knocker's note (their own words, never evidence of truth) and contact (it stays in the inbox); a knocker pseudonym if the same secret was presented (DEC-78).
        Decides
        Pull (admits the material as a capture carrying the knock's receipt), discard, or leave new.
        Can go wrong
        • A knock already pulled answers existed: true with the same capture; a discarded knock cannot be pulled until put back to new (KNOCK_DISCARDED).
        • An unknown knock: NO_SUCH_KNOCK, nothing written (capture R32, built T12).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Unvetted material entering as if vouched: pulling commits the member to nothing more; vouching stays with release (DEC-78 item 1).
        Open here
        • Built at the plane; the setup page's inbox performs the pull ('Mark as taken up'); app.html has no inbox.

        Open capture R32; DEC-78 items 1-4; Intake Doctrine §2a; capture R65; control-plane R36

      3. member · Months or years.
        What is known of the source grows
        Knows
        The source as it stood when the material was received, and each later disclosure, dated and attributed.
        Decides
        Record a disclosure; before publishing any identity detail, ask the source's consent.
        Can go wrong
        • A hostile exposure is recorded as the exposer's claim, never confirmed by the group without the source's consent (DEC-78 item 5(b)).
        • A stored name can be breached or subpoenaed: 'known to the group, not recorded' is a first-class choice (DEC-78 item 5(c)).
        Hands to
        project owner, at publishing (consent)
        Trust risk
        The group making a source more public than they chose (DEC-78 item 5(a)).
        Open here
        • Built at the plane (module sources); no member surface yet. Hand-carried material's intake waits on the upload redesign (K509 (5)).

        Open DEC-78 item 5; sources R1-R15

      (g) A review copy round trip with an outside reviewer · 3 steps
      1. project editors, owner · Hours.
        Editors draft; owner grants a named recipient
        Knows
        What is missing (the publish gates' own answer).
        Decides
        Whom to grant.
        Can go wrong
        • C-87.3 not the owner.
        • Recipient text malformed.
        Hands to
        recipient
        Trust risk
        Advance copy mistaken for a publication.

        Fixed review R3-R8, R13

      2. review recipient · Days.
        Recipient reads, comments, acknowledges the statement
        Knows
        The statement first, then what the case leaves out, then what is still needed.
        Decides
        Comment; acknowledge or attack.
        Can go wrong
        • Grant revoked or draft moved: dead answer.
        • Draft edited after reading: the answer names the latest change (review R17).
        Hands to
        editors
        Trust risk
        The copy changed after the reviewer read it.

        Fixed review R10-R19; Publication section 6A.4

      3. owner · Minutes.
        Editors revise; the owner publishes, naming the draft
        Knows
        Acknowledgements bound to this case identity.
        Decides
        Publish now.
        Can go wrong
        • Readings on a draft not named at publish are counted UNDETERMINED, never listed (Publication section 3 rule 13).
        • No surface sends draft= yet.
        Hands to
        public
        Trust risk
        Attributing a reading to a case its reader never saw.

        Fixed case-authoring R9; Publication section 3 rule 13

      (h) A records request round trip · 3 steps
      1. member · Minutes.
        Make the request under its governing law
        Knows
        The law and its deadline (from the profile).
        Decides
        What to ask; which law.
        Can go wrong
        • MACHINE_CANNOT_STATE_RECORDS_LAW.
        • RECORDS_LAW_REFUSED (not yet enforced).
        Hands to
        the office
        Trust risk
        None named.

        Fixed actions R4-R6, R18

      2. member · Statutory period.
        Record it sent; state the clock
        Knows
        Statutory days, holidays.
        Decides
        Due date with citation.
        Can go wrong
        • LEASE_HELD while another member writes the action.
        Hands to
        plane
        Trust risk
        A computed date stored as fact (never: overdue is derived).

        Fixed actions R15-R16, R32, R35

      3. member · Days to weeks.
        Record what came back: records, fee quote, denial, or nothing
        Knows
        The ledger.
        Decides
        Direction, stage, quote, exemptions.
        Can go wrong
        • QUOTE_NOT_ON_RECEIVED.
        • STAGE_NOT_OF_DIRECTION.
        • No response: recorded as its own entry.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        A non-response read as agreement (a declared inference bias can block that reading).

        Fixed actions R15-R22; Declared Bias (inference example); Case Making section 8

      (i) A source changes after a case is published · 3 steps
      1. plane · On cadence.
        Monitoring detects modified or removed
        Knows
        Baseline and new bytes.
        Decides
        None (mechanical).
        Can go wrong
        • Shell match never reads unchanged; governed refusal is not the source failing.
        Hands to
        members homed under the document
        Trust risk
        Detecting change is mechanical; what it means is not.

        Fixed monitoring R5-R11, R37-R39

      2. member · Days.
        Members see affected findings and version notices
        Knows
        Causes derived on read.
        Decides
        Adopt newer, keep earlier, record re-evaluation.
        Can go wrong
        • Withheld dependents stated without a count.
        • 'Weakened' cause not yet met (reevaluation R17).
        Hands to
        owner
        Trust risk
        Silence claimed without reading ('unaffected').

        Fixed reevaluation R1-R21; queue R9, R12 (built T12, N172); DEC-78 item 5(e)

      3. owner · Days.
        Reopen and publish a new edition
        Knows
        The published edition keeps answering.
        Decides
        Correct forward.
        Can go wrong
        • COMPLETENESS_CARRIED_FORWARD if the statement is byte-identical.
        • Determinations and packets flagged basis_changed.
        Hands to
        public
        Trust risk
        Readers relying on the old edition; supersession is surfaced, not pushed.

        Fixed promotion R21-R26; case-authoring R10; conformance R10; filings R12; DEC-12

      (j) Adding a third administrator, or removing one · 2 steps
      1. administrator · Minutes.
        Propose the new administrator
        Knows
        Current count; votes needed.
        Decides
        Whom to propose.
        Can go wrong
        • Created proposed at two or more administrators.
        Hands to
        other administrators
        Trust risk
        'Pending approval' instead of '2 of 3' hides the arithmetic.

        Fixed membership R5, R14; Interaction Constructs section B

      2. administrators · Days waiting on others.
        Endorse, or vote to remove with a reason
        Knows
        Tally, who must still act, what carries.
        Decides
        Endorse or vote.
        Can go wrong
        • NOT_PROPOSED.
        • ROOT_OF_TRUST (founder cannot be removed; hosting account is the remedy).
        • Impossible at two administrators.
        • A bearer credential cannot cast a vote (control-plane R14).
        Hands to
        none
        Trust risk
        Being outvoted without seeing the denominator.

        Fixed membership R6-R8; control-plane R14

      (k) From suspicion to a closed plan · 12 steps
      1. joined member · Not stated in the sources.
        Open a plan while the inquiry is young
        Knows
        The inquiry is open; nothing is determined or published yet.
        Decides
        A title; whether to plan now.
        Can go wrong
        • MACHINE_CANNOT_PLAN.
        • PLAN_NO_TITLE (empty, over 200 characters, quote, backslash or line break).
        • SUBJECT_IN_ACTIVE_PLAN: the inquiry is already in an open plan of this project (named).
        • SUBJECT_NOT_LIVE: a closed inquiry.
        Hands to
        the plan page (receipt)
        Trust risk
        Planning before anything is established could read as prejudging; the plan shows the subject as hypothetical.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 1; action-plans R1-R3; Bob's ruling 10

      2. member · Not stated in the sources.
        See what the plan rests on
        Knows
        Each subject's support and liveness.
        Decides
        Whether to add subjects (an outcome of a determination, or another inquiry) or wait.
        Can go wrong
        • A subject superseded or closed since: shown by liveness, never silently.
        • A determination recorded since on a suspected subject's act is offered as determined_since; nothing is added without a member.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        A plan that hid a hypothetical premise would be the over-claiming failure; hunch debt is shown at the top.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 2; action-plans R5-R8; OQ-9 whyItMatters

      3. member; the assistant proposes · Not stated in the sources.
        Get suggestions
        Knows
        The subjects, the standards' text, consequences, the project's kind of work, the profile's deadlines, venues and legal organisations, and the same project's earlier plans only.
        Decides
        Which suggestions to adopt; whether to ask for the next five.
        Can go wrong
        • A suggestion read as the group's decision (the tray must never).
        • No assistant suggestions until its plan mode is deployed, as soon as the assistant runs model turns (run-rules R14; K660 (5)); a member may still propose or add options.
        • A cursor the tray did not give: PROPOSALS_CURSOR_REFUSED (action-plans R34).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Suggestions shaped by the declared kind of work could narrow what the group considers; they shape suggestions and nothing else.
        Open here
        • How the tray shows the five strongest first and offers the next five, with no score, rank figure or strength (action-plans R34; K660 (2)): the words and the control are the design's.

        Open PATH.md §2 step 3; action-plans R11, R21, R30-R34; skills R28-R29; agent-worker R50-R53; run-rules R14; Bob, 2026-09-30 (K660)

      4. member (or the assistant fills the form for them to check) · Not stated in the sources.
        Add an option of one's own
        Knows
        Categories; the addressee shapes; regulated dates need a basis.
        Decides
        Summary, category, subjects served, addressee, dates with basis, tier (legal only).
        Can go wrong
        • ADDRESSEE_REFUSED (a private individual, or a shape missing its role or organisation).
        • DATE_REFUSED (no basis).
        • TIER_REFUSED (a tier on a non-legal option).
        • LOBBYING_NO_REQUIREMENT (a lobbying option naming no requirement it enforces).
        • OPTION_KEY_REFUSED (budget, cost, assignee, hours, significance, priority, score).
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Wanting to record cost or who does it; the plan refuses both by design.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 4; action-plans R9-R12, R26

      5. member · Not stated in the sources.
        Read and sort the options
        Knows
        Collapsed summaries, categories, dates; expanded detail and history.
        Decides
        Which to consider together.
        Can go wrong
        • A legal option with no tier reads 'undetermined', never a default.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Venues and legal organisations shown beside legal options could read as recommendations; they are labelled as facts.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 5, §3

      6. member · Not stated in the sources.
        Choose, and say when to be reminded
        Knows
        Defaults for reminders shown and changeable right there.
        Decides
        Which to choose; which to decline and why; when to be reminded of each dated option.
        Can go wrong
        • REMINDER_REFUSED rejects the whole act (all or none).
        • Declining without a reason is refused; undecided needs nothing.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Feeling pushed to decide everything; the page never asks for the unticked ones (DEC-69).
        Open here
        • The default reminder days are the surface's (R29: 'nothing here sets one').

        Open PATH.md §2 step 6; action-plans R13, R29; DEC-94; DEC-77

      7. member · Checkpoints fall 1-3,650 days after their phase starts (action-plans R14); e.g. '8 weeks in'.
        Lay out a scenario
        Knows
        Chosen options; regulated dates; other subjects' tracks.
        Decides
        Phases, their order, checkpoints and branches; up to three scenarios.
        Can go wrong
        • PHASE_OPTION_NOT_CHOSEN; PHASE_CYCLE; BRANCH_UNKNOWN; SCENARIO_OUT_OF_RANGE.
        • Checks: a regulated date before its phase can start; a judgement with no branch for one outcome; no branch answering a hostile response.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        A branch that waits on another subject's track could read as a forecast.
        Open here
        • Side by side or tabs on narrow screens (Design).
        • How a waiting branch is drawn without implying a forecast (Design).

        Open PATH.md §2 step 7, §4; action-plans R14-R15, R19

      8. member (from the queue item) · When its day comes; the queue item waits until a member judges it or the plan closes.
        Judge a checkpoint
        Knows
        The condition in words; the phase's options and how they went.
        Decides
        Met or not met, with a note.
        Can go wrong
        • CHECKPOINT_NOT_DUE; CHECKPOINT_JUDGED (no re-judging).
        Hands to
        the scenario's branch
        Trust risk
        A missed checkpoint read as a finding about the city; it is always the group's own (action-plans R23).

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 8; action-plans R16-R17, R23; queue-producers R16

      9. member; may name the group's contact · Not stated in the sources.
        Start an option
        Knows
        The ACT preview: what the action will say, rest on and refuse.
        Decides
        Start; or, for a breach resting on an unestablished subject, remove the subject, wait, untick 'asserts a breach', or proceed with a stated reason.
        Can go wrong
        • OPTION_NOT_CHOSEN; OPTION_STARTED (names the action); CONTACT_NOT_A_MEMBER.
        • ACTION_NO_DETERMINATION / DETERMINATION_SUPERSEDED unless overridden.
        • ADDRESSEE_NOT_AN_OFFICE for a breach action.
        • An overridden action can never join an escalation (ACTION_PREMISE_OVERRIDDEN).
        Hands to
        the action page (receipt)
        Trust risk
        Proceeding on an unestablished premise is visible to every reader: 'rests on an unestablished premise' on the action and everything prepared from it.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 9; action-plans R18; actions R8-R9; escalation R23; docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md (canon whole, K608 (1)) §4 rule 2

      10. member; the assistant may draft · Not stated in the sources.
        Prepare what is sent
        Knows
        A legal option goes through the filing or counsel-packet surface; any other through 'Draft message'.
        Decides
        Edit and approve the text; send it by the venue's own means; record the sending.
        Can go wrong
        • FILING_STALE; STILL_UNFILLED; KIND_NO_TEMPLATE when neither the profile nor the group has a template.
        • ALREADY_SENT; NOT_APPROVED.
        Hands to
        the addressee (outside)
        Trust risk
        A machine-prepared text mistaken for the group's approved words; approval fixes the bytes and adds the stamp.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 10; filings R1-R7, R22-R26

      11. member; the system reads · The counterparty's window: statutory, or the group's stated one (a request for comment's window is the group's; 7-30 days is a cited precedent, never enforced).
        Track
        Knows
        Each action's state and each subject's escalation stage on 'Started'.
        Decides
        Record replies; answer reminders; judge a proposed stage.
        Can go wrong
        • A deadline passed arrives as a CONDITION once; a proposed stage as an OBLIGATION.
        • A reply may be pressure: marked and kept as evidence.
        Hands to
        member
        Trust risk
        Being nagged, or missing a deadline; reminders are the member's own and nothing repeats unless asked (DEC-94).

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 11; queue-producers R15, R17-R18; actions R44, R48; DEC-94

      12. member · Not stated in the sources.
        Close the action, then the plan
        Knows
        Each action's outcome; whether the group is done.
        Decides
        The resolution (complied, denied, escalated, withdrawn, completed) with its own outcome in words; close the plan with a reason.
        Can go wrong
        • NO_RESOLUTION; PLAN_CLOSED if already closed.
        • Closing changes no action or escalation.
        Hands to
        the group; the plan stays readable, its subjects free for another plan
        Trust risk
        Closing too early; nothing closes a plan but a member.

        Fixed PATH.md §2 step 12; actions R7, R26; action-plans R20

      Screens and their rules

      The rules for each main screen: what it must show, what it must never show or do, and every state it must be designed for. Then the full list of screens the requirements imply, and whether the old interface has each one.

      Queue (the attention layer)
      • Things that want me, grouped by the case they belong to; the one way work reaches a person. Interaction Constructs revision 0.2; DEC-10

      Must show

      • Class of each item: obligation (do / forward / resolve), queue finding (adopt / defer / dismiss), condition (status or mute). Fixed queue R1, R12; NOTIFICATIONS.md
      • A queue finding must look derived: its source and derivation, and that nobody has judged it yet. Fixed queue R16; D-82
      • Agent-surfaced marker wherever a question appears. Fixed ai-runs R27; System Design row 12 (D-82)
      • Resolved by X on this date, for items someone else resolved. Fixed queue R39; DEC-16
      • What my mutes and snoozes are suppressing, with an undo. Fixed queue R14, R40; System Design row 12 (UI-97)
      • Each option's rung and set weight, pre-flighted for this caller. Fixed queue R12, R17; affordances R11
      • An obligation's own resolving act, as the item publishes it. Fixed queue R12; NOTIFICATIONS.md (REC-207)
      • The finding class labelled 'Noticed'. Fixed K356; queue R1 (built T12, N301)
      • A record contradiction as a duty that cannot be muted or dismissed, leaving only by resolution; a world one as a lead dismissed in one act with a fixed reason. Fixed DEC-76 item 3; DEC-84 (1)-(2), (17)
      • A newer capture that affects a member's reference, with adopt and keep. Fixed queue R9, R12 (built T12, N172)
      • Each obligation's 'instead' names its own door: signerset for a self-registered key, biasdebtresolve for a bias debt, taskresolve otherwise. Fixed queue R12; K607
      • A conflict with a record the member cannot see, homed only from the member's own side (contradiction-duty-unseen, contradiction-plurality-unseen). Fixed queue-producers R7; DEC-85
      • Whose it is: the group's own (a checkpoint, marked 'ours') or the counterparty's (a deadline: 'the counterparty's deadline, not ours'). Fixed PATH.md §3; build/plan/action-design/surfaces.html (design view, not stated as approved)
      • A checkpoint due: the plan, scenario and phase, and its condition; judged met or not met with a note. Fixed queue-producers R16; action-plans R16
      • A proposed stage: 'a member advances or declines with a reason; the system never advances'. Fixed queue-producers R17; build/plan/action-design/surfaces.html (design view, not stated as approved)
      • A reminder the member asked for, to that member only; its answer offers another reminder on a later day or none. Fixed queue-producers R18; action-clocks R6; DEC-10 (D-125); DEC-94 (1)
      • An overdue deadline as a CONDITION naming the entry's date, basis and text, raised once. Fixed queue-producers R15; K611; DEC-94 (4)
      • As a deadline nears, position, colour or wording may change: display, not a notification. Fixed DEC-94 (2)

      Must never show or do

      • Never nag: one standing entry per (member, case); re-notify only on snooze expiry or something new. Fixed Interaction Constructs DEC-10; DEC-69
      • Never present a machine finding as an obligation; one check across 58 contracts is one proposal with 58 instances. Fixed Interaction Constructs section P (PROPOSAL); D-79
      • Never let a mute look like a record act, or a dismissal look personal. Fixed queue R14-R15, R20
      • Never mute an obligation. Fixed queue R19 (KIND_NOT_PERSONAL)
      • Never a severity ladder of warning / error colours in place of the three classes. Fixed NOTIFICATIONS.md 'three, not four severities'
      • Never reveal a task on a subject the viewer may not see: it reads as no task. Fixed tasks R6; K565
      • A missed or not-met checkpoint as a finding, a condition of the record or a fact about the government. Fixed action-plans R23; PATH.md §3
      • Repeat anything unless the member asked; 'due within N days' is not new unless requested. Fixed DEC-69; DEC-70; DEC-94 (4); action-clocks R8
      • Any outside channel (email, push). Fixed DEC-94 (3)

      States to design

      • Undetermined:
        • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
      • Withheld:
        • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
      • Refused:
        • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
      • Truncated:
        • Every list says when it was cut at a stated bound. Fixed inventory display primitive 'Truncated / bound'; queue R6; membership R48
      • Empty:
        • What does an empty queue say (nothing wants you, versus nothing could be read)? Open
      • Loading:
        • Not specified. Open
      • Stale:
        • A snoozed case's items are marked snoozed until the instant, not withheld by the plane. Fixed queue R40
      • Conflict:
        • Another member resolved a shared item: show attribution, not a gap. Fixed Interaction Constructs DEC-16
      • Ungrouped:
        • Items with no case sit ungrouped; a home set that is undetermined is stated. Fixed queue R7

      Primary acts

      • resolve an obligation (taskresolve) affordances R1, R27
      • forward an obligation (taskforward) affordances R1, R27
      • defer / dismiss a queue finding (proposedispose) affordances R1-R2
      • mute / snooze queue R20-R21
      • checkpointrecord Open queue R12; affordances R3; K732
      • escalationadvance / escalationdecline Fixed affordances R2; queue R12
      • reminderanswer Fixed queue R12; action-clocks R6; affordances R3; K732

      Access and language

      • Phones supported, acceptably as a viewing MVP; features may be absent on some devices. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (devices)
      • Plain, reader-language meaning for every state, from one semantics table; every chip is a click-over disclosing its row. Fixed UI-KICKOFF state-semantics table
      • No accessibility standard (screen reader, keyboard, contrast) is named in canon; the UI plan records the hardening pass as absent. What level is required? Open
      • No language other than English is named in canon. Must the product or its published output be multilingual? Open

      queue; Interaction Constructs; NOTIFICATIONS.md; PATH.md §1, §3; queue R1, R5, R12; queue-producers R15-R18

      ACT (the one motion for doing something to a record or a set)
      • Choose; see what it will refuse and why before it runs; author the reason; get a receipt. Interaction Constructs revision 0.2 (ACT)

      Must show

      • Every act's button carries its rung's name and weight pips. Friction follows the rung: reversible inline; reasoned a reason field in place; terminal and attested a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs; irreversible only through the ceremony. Fixed affordances R2; DEC-87; Interaction Constructs §F
      • For a set: whether the act is all-or-nothing, report-and-proceed, or per-item, before acting. Fixed Interaction Constructs section S
      • The ballot's denominator: '2 of 3', never 'pending approval'; the divergence at two owners shown. Fixed Interaction Constructs section B
      • The receipt: what was written, by whom, when; a preview answers with nothing written. Fixed basis-versions R14
      • An act whose effect cannot be taken back outside the record opens the full dialog stating that effect, whatever its rung: attributing a name, sharing a lead, naming a person in the registry, setting the group bar. Fixed DEC-88 item 4
      • A recommendation, labelled machine work, is shown first with its reason and accepted in one attributed act; facts the record holds are filled in with their source. Fixed DEC-77 item 3

      Must never show or do

      • Never compute a refusal; only render one received from the plane (publication or dry run). Fixed Interaction Constructs RULED 2026-08-01 (DEC-8)
      • Never prefill, suggest, template or complete a justification; assembling the member's own prior words is allowed. Fixed Interaction Constructs section J (boundary of the prefill rule)
      • Never land an act on rows the member did not see; nothing preselected; visibility may only shrink a selection. Fixed Interaction Constructs section S (D-35, DEC-69)
      • Never offer an act the caller's capability lacks (absent, not greyed). Fixed Membership v2 section 5
      • Never one dialog for every act with the rung merely highlighted. Fixed DEC-87; Interaction Constructs revision 0.2
      • Never enter a value the member never saw under their name; nothing silently preselected. Fixed DEC-77 item 3(c)

      States to design

      • Undetermined:
        • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
      • Withheld:
        • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
      • Refused:
        • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
      • Truncated:
        • Every list says when it was cut at a stated bound. Fixed inventory display primitive 'Truncated / bound'; queue R6; membership R48
      • Empty:
        • Not specified. Open
      • Loading:
        • Not specified. Open
      • Stale:
        • A selection whose members moved: report says what moved; refuse hands over nothing (SET_MOVED). Fixed retrieval R19-R20
      • Conflict:
        • A second member changed the object first: CAS_STALE naming expected and got; never merged. An action's 30-second lease held by another: LEASE_HELD. Fixed promotion R1; actions R16
      • Expired:
        • A selection handle unknown, released or expired: NO_SUCH_SELECTION, one answer. Fixed retrieval R19
      • No rung yet:
        • An act whose rung is not yet ruled is treated as reasoned. Fixed DEC-87

      Primary acts

      • any reasoned act affordances R1-R2

      Access and language

      • Phones supported, acceptably as a viewing MVP; features may be absent on some devices. Fixed UI-KICKOFF refinements (devices)
      • Plain, reader-language meaning for every state, from one semantics table; every chip is a click-over disclosing its row. Fixed UI-KICKOFF state-semantics table
      • No accessibility standard (screen reader, keyboard, contrast) is named in canon; the UI plan records the hardening pass as absent. What level is required? Open
      • No language other than English is named in canon. Must the product or its published output be multilingual? Open
      • Labels never use AND, OR, disjunction, branch or ground when structuring support. Fixed affordances R5 (DEC-32 clause 1)

      Interaction Constructs; affordances; DEC-87, DEC-88 (friction by rung)

      Question workspace (the inquiry page)
      • Where a member builds a question's support, compares accounts and concludes. Case Making; basis-versions; inquiry

      Must show

      • Legs with role (supports / cuts against), earned grade, what it was capped from and why; ungraded legs named as not load-bearing. Fixed inquiry R13-R14, R32; strength R3
      • The strength pair per axis (capture, connection) with testimony beside; unrated and undetermined stated; what-ifs labelled as exploration. Fixed strength R1-R10, R18-R20
      • Versions, their states, which is CURRENT per project, and each project's conclusion history. Fixed basis-versions R5, R11, R22
      • Structure authored before strength is shown; restructuring after seeing is recorded and attributed. Fixed DECISIONS.md DEC-32 enactment note (UI-27)
      • A per-leg control asking whether a newer capture holds the passage. Fixed System Design row 4 (UI-96); content R29
      • Who reopened, divided or set aside the question, when and why. Fixed inquiry R19, R24
      • Machine-suggested versions labelled as machine work, with the run that made them. Fixed AI Roles rule 3; run-productions R5
      • Each leg's grade on the one A-D scale with its scale's icon; hover names the weakest document or link and how to raise it; a click highlights that leg. Fixed DEC-82; Interaction Constructs §M
      • A tension mark wherever two conflicting points sit together (supporting and cutting-against legs, alternative versions, two projects' conclusions), opening the 'how do these differ?' clarifier. Fixed DEC-77 item 1; DEC-84 (3)

      Must never show or do

      • Never a single confidence score or a case-level strength. Fixed AI Roles rule 9; strength R18
      • Never draft framing or a conclusion for the member. Fixed Case Making section 4a
      • Never let one project's act move another project's stance. Fixed basis-versions R31; inquiry R39
      • Never hide grades: the old interface's grade-free workspace (UI-16) was an interface choice, superseded; never one combined badge. Fixed DEC-82; DEC-44

      States to design

      • Undetermined:
        • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
      • Withheld:
        • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
      • Refused:
        • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
      • Truncated:
        • Every list says when it was cut at a stated bound. Fixed inventory display primitive 'Truncated / bound'; queue R6; membership R48
      • Empty:
        • A question with no legs: what does it invite, without nagging? Open
      • Loading:
        • Not specified. Open
      • Stale:
        • Stale / newer version notice on a leg: affected or undetermined. Fixed content R22; reevaluation R14
      • Conflict:
        • Two projects concluded differently: both stand, each attributed; conclusions that cannot both hold in the same respect carry a tension mark, a named difference recorded on both clears it, and otherwise it is a duty on both projects, neither made to adopt the other's answer. Fixed basis-versions R31; DEC-84 (3)
      • Contradiction:
        • A tension mark opens the clarifier: different time or occasion, part or scope, meaning, observer or method, not the same subject; one of them is wrong; they really conflict; not sure. Only the last two open the contradiction inquiry. Explained without evidence, the mark softens and does not clear. Fixed DEC-77 item 1; DEC-84 (16)

      inquiry; basis-versions; strength; citation

      Tension mark, clarifier and contradiction inquiry
      • Meet a conflict where its two points already sit, settle it there in one act when possible, and escalate to the dedicated contradiction inquiry only when it will not dissolve. DEC-77 item 1; DEC-76 item 2

      Must show

      • Both sides in the record's own words, each with source, date, document type and why the two were paired (the compare view). Fixed DEC-77 item 2
      • The clarifier's choices: different time or occasion, part or scope, meaning, observer or method, not the same subject (multi-select); one of them is wrong; they really conflict; not sure. Fixed DEC-77 item 1
      • Facts the record holds filled in with their source; a recommendation of which respects may differ, labelled machine work, shown first with its reason. Fixed DEC-77 item 3; DEC-84 (5)
      • In the contradiction inquiry: the assumptions checklist as its working view, the timeline for a reversal, the competing-hypotheses matrix for a conflict that will not dissolve or a conflict of norms. Fixed DEC-77 item 2; K447 point 9
      • The mark's state wherever a side appears: in tension, explained not yet shown (softened), stale with its reason (naming the member, instant and act), qualified, held irreconcilable, lead, plurality, unseen conflict. Fixed DEC-77 items 1-2; DEC-84 (7); contradiction R27; K544

      Must never show or do

      • Never say which side is wrong, or that a conflict is genuine, on the machine's word. Fixed DEC-84 (5); DEC-24
      • Never preselect a choice; never fill the member's reason from the machine's. Fixed DEC-77 item 3(c); DEC-84 (14)
      • Never let a record duty be muted, dismissed or set aside; never mark aspirations as in contradiction (they are in contact). Fixed DEC-84 (2); DEC-77 item 1
      • Never show a precision or unrelated label as a tension, and never reveal a side the viewer may not see. Fixed CONTRADICTION-PRESENT-RESOLVE-DESIGN.md §4; contradiction R10
      • Never name or count a project the viewer cannot see in a candidate's reach; a hidden project reads as absent. Fixed contradiction R55; K523, K544
      • Never offer 'conclude' on a contradiction inquiry; offer 'Resolve this contradiction' only to a viewer who sees both sides. Fixed affordances R8, R14; K516

      States to design

      • Undetermined:
        • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
      • Withheld:
        • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
      • Refused:
        • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
      • Softened:
        • Explained without evidence on the screen: the mark softens and does not clear; a record duty stays in the queue. Fixed DEC-77 item 1; DEC-76 item 1
      • Stale:
        • A side marked wrong: stale with its reason, never deleted; holders get a re-evaluation notice. Fixed DEC-84 (7), (16)
      • Empty:
        • Not specified. Open
      • Loading:
        • Not specified. Open

      Primary acts

      • clarify, how the two differ (contradictionclarify) contradiction R32; affordances R2
      • one of them is wrong (contradictionclarify, one_wrong) contradiction R33; DEC-84 (16)
      • take up as a contradiction inquiry (contradictiontakeup) contradiction R35; affordances R2
      • accept a recommendation (the acceptance basis of clarify or resolve) contradiction R30; DEC-77 item 3(b)
      • dismiss a lead, one fixed reason (contradictiondismiss) contradiction R31; DEC-84 (17)
      • resolve the contradiction inquiry with its kind (contradictionresolve) contradiction R36; affordances R2, R8
      • ask to resolve a conflict with a record you cannot see (contradictionoptin) / respond (contradictionrespond) contradiction R51, R53; affordances R3

      • Its source (who handed it over, or the unnamed knocker) apart from the member who captured it. Fixed DEC-78 item 2

      • The attestation attempts made at capture, late attestations ('proves the bytes existed by <at>, not at capture'), and the capturing member's signed accounts to those who may see the capture's bundle. Fixed capture R20, R68, R69; K580
      • For a doorbell document: 'received, not fetched', the member's authored letter stated as authored, and the knock's receipt as its chain. Fixed provenance R19, R42, R51; K538, K581

      case-authoring; Publication

      Publication ceremony
      • The heaviest act and the only way to it: five steps (what becomes permanent; what this rests on; what you are leaving out; the edition this creates; sign), after a pre-flight that runs the real refusals first. It replaces the placeholder. DEC-80; Publication §5

      Must show

      • What is about to become permanent, and what it will and will not claim (bytes, address, date, route; not authenticity). Fixed Interaction Constructs section A
      • An explicit key act; signer and deliverer as two facts. Fixed Interaction Constructs revision 0.2; ratification R12
      • Correction moves forward: a new edition, never erasure. Fixed affordances R2 (IRREVERSIBLE_CORRECTION_PATH); DEC-19
      • Before the first screen, the pre-flight's real refusals, writing nothing: an uncleared hunch first, below the bar, no attesting key (with a remedy naming self-registration). Fixed DEC-80 item 3; case-authoring R12, R34; ratification R18
      • Step three: exclusions and their acknowledgements, what was searched, the bias acknowledged, any unresolved record contradiction for the owner to disclose, and consent for any source identity the case would reveal. Fixed DEC-80 item 2; DEC-76 item 4; DEC-78 item 5
      • Each document's capture grade and whether it is co-attested; a missing co-attestation retried late first (reattest), then acknowledged with a reason and marked 'self-attested only', with the capturing member's signed accounts. Fixed DEC-81 items 1, 3; case-authoring R35-R36; capture R68-R69
      • Signing in the browser with a key the owner registered themself or an administrator registered, confirmed by passphrase or device unlock; any key origin verifies. Fixed DEC-80 item 4; ratification R19; membership R89-R91
      • Each tension in one of four sentences ('In tension, not yet resolved', 'Explained, not yet shown', 'Held irreconcilable by the group', 'Rests on a side in conflict with a record not shown'); a conflict whose other side the owner cannot see is told as such, and the owner is told it will be highlighted in the case. Fixed case-authoring R31-R33; DEC-85
      • The publish act carries the self-attested prompt; recording a source's consent carries the consent prompt. Fixed affordances R28, R29

      Must never show or do

      • Never a button in a row of buttons; the ceremony is the safeguard. Fixed Interaction Constructs revision 0.2 'Where FEWER costs something'
      • Never let a machine credential or bearer token deliver an attested act. Fixed AI Roles rule 4 (D-421)
      • Never discover NO_SIGNERS only after signing. Fixed Interaction Constructs RULED 2026-08-01
      • Never refuse a case because a contradiction exists; refuse only one left undisclosed. Fixed DEC-76 item 4; DEC-84 (13)
      • Never refuse outright a document that cannot be co-archived. Fixed DEC-81 item 3(d)

      Publication section 6A; review

      Standards, determinations, consequences, actions and escalation
      • Where the group measures a government act against standards and pursues it to compliance restored and consequences addressed. layer 9 modules; Design Requirement 7; Operational Principle 6
      • On the action page: one outward engagement and everything prepared for it. actions Purpose; PATH.md §3
      • On the matter page (design view, not stated as approved): one government act: determination per standard, consequences, escalation, attached actions and pressure, on one page. build/plan/action-design/matter-page.html (design view, not stated as approved)

      Must show

      • Each standard's own captured words and whether it was in force on a date (undetermined stated). Fixed standards R2, R7
      • Outcomes per standard, never one verdict; findings with their frozen pairs. Fixed conformance R4, R9
      • Consequence parts by state (computed, assessed, undetermined), causation established or unproven, totals only within a state. Fixed consequences R2-R7
      • Each action's tier (1, 2, 3 or undetermined), counterparty by role and body, governing laws, ledger, clocks with their statute and overdue derived. Fixed actions R25, R35
      • Escalation stage, each proposed edge with its trigger and what is missing, declines and their age; exit conditions separately. Fixed escalation R2-R3, R16
      • basis_changed flags naming causes. Fixed conformance R10; consequences R8; filings R12
      • Why the group is pursuing a breach (the opening reason), or why it declined to (a reasoned 'decline to escalate'), in the member's own words; whether each determination was escalated, declined or neither. Fixed DEC-89
      • For an obligation-against-act finding: Criteria, Condition, Cause (only when evidenced, else 'cause not established'), Effect, Recommendation (a proposed action). Fixed DEC-77 item 2; DEC-84 (10)
      • A comparison started from a contradiction inquiry: the member names which side states the standard (never defaulted); each side with its source, date and text, labelled the record's; outcomes_differ stated quietly when per-standard outcomes differ. Fixed conformance R9, R12, R21, R22; K503, K569
      • On the action page: the plan and option it came from. Fixed PATH.md §3; actions R46
      • On the action page: the group's contact, if named. Fixed PATH.md §3; actions R45
      • On the action page: the addressee by role and organisation (or a described audience). Fixed PATH.md §3; actions R9
      • On the action page: its own outcome in words when resolved, including 'completed' ('the story ran on 14 October'). Fixed actions R7, R26; PATH.md §2 step 12
      • On the action page: pressure entries apart, each kept as evidence and able to open an inquiry. Fixed actions R48; build/plan/action-design/matter-page.html (design view, not stated as approved)
      • On the action page: the member's reminders on its dated entries: entry, day, who set it, state. Fixed action-clocks R4
      • On the matter page (design view, not stated as approved): per standard: what it requires, what the act did, and the outcome; a compliant outcome with the same care.       Action plan (the plan page, S11)
      • A project's working material for deciding what to do about one or more matters, suspected or determined; an outline, not a graph (a graph view may come later). action-plans Purpose; UX-ANSWERS.md OQ-9; Bob's rulings 1-10 (K590)

      Must show

      • Four parts, top to bottom: Subjects, Options, Scenarios, Started. Fixed PATH.md §1; build/plan/action-design/plan-page.html (approved by Bob, K608 (4))
      • The checks in words under the title, each linking to what it is about: a regulated date past or before its phase can start; a judgement with no branch for one outcome; an option bound only to subjects no longer live; an outward option bound only to hypothetical subjects; a scenario with no branch answering a hostile response; a lobbying option whose target is superseded. A check informs and never refuses. Fixed action-plans R19; PATH.md §1
      • Each subject's support in words (established, short, 'hypothetical: not yet shown') and its liveness; a plan resting only on hypothetical subjects says so at the top, as hunch debt. Fixed action-plans R6, R8; PATH.md §2 step 2, §3
      • Each option's category, subjects served, regulated dates with their basis, disposition and origin (member or assistant). Fixed PATH.md §3; action-plans R6, R9, R11
      • Options collapsed to summary, category and dates; expanded: detail, addressee, subjects served, where it came from and its history; sortable by category, regulated start or end date, subject or disposition. Fixed PATH.md §2 step 5, §3
      • A declined option keeps its reason and stays visible. Fixed action-plans R13; Bob's ruling 7
      • Legal options show the profile's venues and legal organisations beside them, labelled as facts, never recommendations. Fixed PATH.md §2 step 5; filings R21; Bob's ruling 2
      • A legal option's tier written out; 'undetermined' when unstated. Fixed action-plans R9; PATH.md §3
      • Scenarios (up to three) as phases in order, each with its start words, its checkpoint and its branches; a branch waiting on another subject's track. Fixed action-plans R14-R15; PATH.md §2 step 7
      • Started: each started option's action and its state, and each determined subject's escalation stage. Fixed action-plans R6; PATH.md §2 step 11
      • The project's declared kind of work. Fixed action-plans R6, R21
      • Its history: every act, oldest first, with who, when and why. Fixed action-plans R6

      Must never show or do

      • Never a cost, budget, assignee, hours, score or priority. Fixed action-plans R26; PATH.md §3; Bob's ruling 3
      • Never a suggestion inside the options before a member adopts it. Fixed PATH.md §3; action-plans R11
      • Never ask the member to decide unticked options; undecided needs no act. Fixed PATH.md §2 step 6; Bob's ruling 7; DEC-69
      • Never judge a checkpoint or close the plan by machine; a plan never closes itself. Fixed action-plans R16, R20, R24
      • Never published or reachable from any public read. Fixed action-plans R25; DEC-25
      • Never present a fixed catalogue of responses. Fixed Bob's ruling 2; docs/architecture/BIO_Action_v0_1.md (canon whole, K608 (1)) §4 rule 8

      States to design

      • Undetermined:
        • One component with a fixed mark and a mandatory 'because…' line, the same everywhere; never an error, never a zero. Fixed DEC-86; Interaction Constructs §U
      • Withheld:
        • Something exists the viewer may not see: its own treatment and one fixed sentence stating the incompleteness, never identity, title, state or count. Fixed DEC-86; DEC-36; queue R7
      • Refused:
        • The plane's refusal in its own words with offenders named: the shared sentence first, then the act's own next step or alternative; never 'something went wrong'. Fixed DEC-83; DEC-86; DEC-49; AI Roles rule 10
      • Truncated:
        • Every list says when it was cut at a stated bound. Fixed inventory display primitive 'Truncated / bound'; action-plans R7 (200 per page)
      • Empty:
        • A plan just opened holds its first subject (the inquiry, hypothetical) and no options; its empty-state wording is not specified. Open action-plans R1-R2
      • Loading:
        • Not specified. Open
      • Stale:
        • Liveness is derived when read: a subject superseded (naming its successor), closed or removed; an option bound only to subjects no longer live says so. Fixed action-plans R8
      • HunchDebt:
        • A plan resting only on hypothetical subjects says so at the top. Fixed PATH.md §2 step 2
      • Closed:
        • A closed plan stays readable; its subjects are free for another plan. Fixed action-plans R20

      How heavy each act is

      Every act carries a weight, its rung, and the rung decides how much friction the act gets (Bob, 2026-09-29, DEC-87). Friction is kept as low as possible so the tool fades and the work stays in focus, yet a heavier act slows the member down, even for a moment, before it is taken.

      • Can be undone happens inline.
      • Needs a stated reason opens a reason field in place.
      • Ends something and Signed, on the record open a full dialog stating what ends or cannot be silently undone, and who signs.
      • Cannot be undone only through the publication ceremony (DEC-80).

      Every act's button shows its rung's name and weight mark (DEC-82). Weights come from the affordances module and, for the 57 acts that had none, from Bob's grades (DEC-88: 26 reversible, 29 reasoned, 2 terminal), shown here at those rungs; the module takes them up when BOB moves them. Where an act's weight is proposed but not yet assigned in the code, it says so. 25 acts here show Weight not yet set: no operation of their own has been located, or they are decided but not yet built and not yet weighed; each is treated as needing a stated reason until BOB assigns it.

      Six acts are judgement calls that carry high friction whatever their rung, because friction follows the consequence in the world, and a heavy consequence is not a new rung (DEC-88):

      • Choose attribution for a case edition (Firsthand observation, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a name that becomes permanent at publication.
      • Share to a project (Lead, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a disclosure that cannot be un-read.
      • Register a subject (Subject, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a person named in the registry.
      • Set the group default bar (Strength, Needs a stated reason): opens the full dialog stating its effect, a gate on the whole group's work.
      • Have the assistant work the objective (Objective, progress and gaps, Needs a stated reason): High friction, the lightest of the six (DEC-88): its reason field opens in place with the run's budget and scope shown beside it.
      • Proceed on an unestablished premise (override, with a reason) (Action, Needs a stated reason): Full dialog (DEC-88 consequence-in-the-world rule)
      • Approve (Filing draft and counsel packet, Ends something): terminal, so the full dialog stating what it ends is already its own.
      • Start a chosen option (creates the action) (Option, Weight not yet set): Full dialog (the ACT preview) whatever its rung: DEC-88's rule that an act that is a step toward something leaving the group opens the full dialog stating that effect; PATH.md §2 step 9 shows what the action will say, what it rests on and what it would refuse before anything runs
      • Proceed on an unestablished premise (override, with a reason) (Option, Needs a stated reason): Full dialog: the disclosure 'Rests on an unestablished premise' travels with the action and everything prepared from it (filings R24) and is visible to every reader (canon §4 rule 2); DEC-88's consequence-in-the-world rule
      • Approve the text (Communication draft, Ends something): terminal, the full dialog stating that approving fixes the bytes and adds the stamp

      An act that is decided but not yet built, such as “Decline to escalate” (DEC-89), is listed at its rung and marked as not yet built.
